# Daily Summary: 2025-03-17

### ASIDE: Architectural Separation of Instructions and Data in Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10566v1)
- **Authors**: Egor Zverev, Evgenii Kortukov, Alexander Panfilov, Soroush Tabesh, Alexandra Volkova, Sebastian Lapuschkin, Wojciech Samek, Christoph H. Lampert
- **Abstract**: Despite their remarkable performance, large language models lack elementary safety features, and this makes them susceptible to numerous malicious attacks. In particular, previous work has identified the absence of an intrinsic separation between instructions and data as a root cause for the success of prompt injection attacks. In this work, we propose an architectural change, ASIDE, that allows the model to clearly separate between instructions and data by using separate embeddings for them. Instead of training the embeddings from scratch, we propose a method to convert an existing model to ASIDE form by using two copies of the original model's embeddings layer, and applying an orthogonal rotation to one of them. We demonstrate the effectiveness of our method by showing (1) highly increased instruction-data separation scores without a loss in model capabilities and (2) competitive results on prompt injection benchmarks, even without dedicated safety training. Additionally, we study the working mechanism behind our method through an analysis of model representations.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "ASIDE: Architectural Separation of Instructions and Data in Language Models":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ASIDE, a novel architectural modification for large language models (LLMs) designed to enforce a clear separation between instructions and data.  The core idea involves using separate embedding layers for tokens identified as either instructions or data.  Instead of training these embeddings from scratch, the authors propose converting existing models by copying the original embedding layer and applying an orthogonal rotation to one copy.  This effectively creates distinct representation spaces for instructions and data.  The authors demonstrate improved instruction-data separation scores, competitive performance on prompt injection benchmarks (even without dedicated safety training), and analyze the mechanism by studying model representations.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty (Score: 7):** The central idea of architecturally enforced instruction-data separation is valuable. While the concept of separating concerns in LLMs isn't entirely new, the specific implementation using duplicated and rotated embedding layers is a novel and pragmatic approach. The rotation technique is interesting because it allows leveraging pre-trained embeddings, significantly reducing the training overhead of introducing this architectural change. The method for initializing with a rotated copy of the original embeddings is also innovative.

*   **Significance (Score: 7):**  The problem of prompt injection and LLM security is a critical issue. ASIDE offers a potential mitigation strategy that could be integrated into existing models with relatively little overhead compared to training from scratch. The empirical results, demonstrating improved separation scores and robustness against prompt injection, suggest the approach could be useful in real-world deployments. Furthermore, the interpretability analysis through feature activation provides valuable insights into how the model differentiates between instructions and data, which can guide further research.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Pragmatic and Efficient:** ASIDE's design focuses on ease of integration into existing LLMs, reducing the need for computationally expensive retraining. The idea of reusing and adapting pre-trained embeddings is a significant advantage.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The paper provides quantitative results on both separation scores and prompt injection benchmarks, strengthening the claims about the effectiveness of the method. The AlpacaEval scores show that utility is preserved, too.
    *   **Interpretability Analysis:** The study of linear separability and instruction feature activation provides some understanding of how ASIDE changes the model's internal representations.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluations:** The experiments cover several different models and attack types, making the results generalizable.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Fine-Tuning Data:**  Using only a standard Alpaca dataset for fine-tuning, without any specific adversarial examples, might not fully reveal ASIDE's potential under stronger attack scenarios.  More extensive adversarial training experiments could reveal limitations.
    *   **Rotation Angle Sensitivity:** While the authors provide an ablation study on the rotation angle, a more in-depth exploration of the optimal rotation strategy could further improve performance.
    *   **Scalability and Generalization:** The experiments primarily focus on the Llama family of models.  Further studies are needed to evaluate ASIDE's effectiveness with other LLM architectures and on larger, more complex datasets. While the authors claim the technique is agnostic to the tokenization method, there might be unexpected interactions with more sophisticated tokenization schemes.
    *   **No theoretical proof:** There's no theorem provided that shows that this method works, although this is often less necessary if empirical results are outstanding.

*   **Potential Influence:**  ASIDE has the potential to influence future research on LLM security and architecture. It provides a concrete approach for addressing instruction-data separation, a problem acknowledged as critical to LLM safety. It also opens avenues for further investigation into how architectural modifications can enhance LLM robustness without sacrificing performance. The interpretability techniques used in the paper could inspire other researchers to explore the inner workings of LLMs more deeply.

*   **Overall:** The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field of LLM security. Its pragmatic approach, empirical validation, and interpretability analysis make it a compelling piece of work. While there are areas for improvement, the potential for influence on future research is significant.

### Autoregressive Image Generation with Randomized Parallel Decoding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10568v1)
- **Authors**: Haopeng Li, Jinyue Yang, Guoqi Li, Huan Wang
- **Abstract**: We introduce ARPG, a novel visual autoregressive model that enables randomized parallel generation, addressing the inherent limitations of conventional raster-order approaches, which hinder inference efficiency and zero-shot generalization due to their sequential, predefined token generation order. Our key insight is that effective random-order modeling necessitates explicit guidance for determining the position of the next predicted token. To this end, we propose a novel guided decoding framework that decouples positional guidance from content representation, encoding them separately as queries and key-value pairs. By directly incorporating this guidance into the causal attention mechanism, our approach enables fully random-order training and generation, eliminating the need for bidirectional attention. Consequently, ARPG readily generalizes to zero-shot tasks such as image inpainting, outpainting, and resolution expansion. Furthermore, it supports parallel inference by concurrently processing multiple queries using a shared KV cache. On the ImageNet-1K 256 benchmark, our approach attains an FID of 1.94 with only 64 sampling steps, achieving over a 20-fold increase in throughput while reducing memory consumption by over 75% compared to representative recent autoregressive models at a similar scale.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Autoregressive Image Generation with Randomized Parallel Decoding":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ARPG (Autoregressive Image Generation with Randomized Parallel Decoding), a novel autoregressive model for image generation. ARPG addresses limitations of conventional raster-scan autoregressive models, which are inefficient and struggle with zero-shot generalization (e.g., inpainting, outpainting). The key idea is "guided decoding," which decouples positional guidance from content representation. Positional information is encoded as learnable, data-independent queries, dynamically shifted using positional embeddings to target specific tokens. The model supports fully random token order training and generation via causal attention.  It enables parallel inference by processing multiple queries concurrently, leveraging a shared KV cache. Experiments demonstrate competitive generation quality and improved efficiency (throughput, memory consumption) on ImageNet, along with strong zero-shot generalization capabilities.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The core idea of guided decoding to achieve random-order autoregressive image generation appears novel. Decoupling position and content and using a learned query-based mechanism is a significant departure from standard raster-scan and masked modeling approaches.  The approach of training with fully random orders and parallelizing the decoding through a KV cache is also innovative. However, the general concept of random-order modeling and parallel decoding has been explored previously (e.g., MaskGIT, RandAR), so the novelty resides primarily in the specific architectural and training choices.

* **Significance:** The significance lies in the potential to overcome the inherent limitations of raster-scan AR models. Higher inference efficiency is a welcome improvement, especially for high-resolution image generation. The zero-shot generalization capability is valuable, extending the applicability of AR models to tasks beyond straightforward image generation. The demonstrated results on ImageNet are competitive with existing approaches. The reported improvement in throughput and memory consumption compared to related methods is impressive.
* **Strengths:**
    * **Efficient Inference:** Parallel decoding through batched query processing and a shared KV cache appears to lead to a substantial improvement in inference speed and memory usage.
    * **Zero-Shot Generalization:** Demonstrating capabilities such as inpainting, outpainting, and resolution expansion without task-specific training is a significant advantage.
    * **Competitive Results:** Achieves a strong FID score on ImageNet 256x256, demonstrating its effectiveness in standard image generation.
    * **Clear Problem Definition:**  The paper clearly articulates the limitations of existing AR models and how ARPG addresses these shortcomings.
    * **Well-Supported Claims:**  The claims are supported by a reasonable amount of empirical evidence, including comparisons with existing methods and ablation studies.
* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Complexity:** The architecture is arguably more complex than standard autoregressive models.  While it claims to avoid memory/computation overhead as compared to alternatives, there is additional engineering overhead to decouple content from position.
    * **Limited Text-to-Image Experimentation:** The paper focuses on class-conditional image generation and does not explore text-to-image generation on a large scale. Text to image is the dominant paradigm of image generation so not addressing it limits the paper's broader impact.
    * **Incremental Improvement:**  While the gains in efficiency are substantial, the improvement in generation quality (FID score) compared to state-of-the-art diffusion models isn't revolutionary. It's more of an incremental improvement, albeit in an important direction. The zero-shot capabilities are showcased but could be more extensively benchmarked.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in autoregressive image generation by providing a more efficient and flexible alternative to raster-scan methods. The guided decoding framework could inspire new architectures for handling sequential data with non-causal dependencies. However, diffusion models remain dominant, so for this paper to have a substantial impact on the field requires further advancements in its generation quality.

**Score:** 7

**Rationale:** ARPG represents a significant advance in autoregressive image generation. It offers a novel solution to the limitations of raster-scan methods through guided decoding, enabling both efficient parallel inference and zero-shot generalization. The empirical results validate the benefits of the approach. However, the paper's impact is somewhat limited by the incremental nature of the generation quality improvements compared to state-of-the-art diffusion models and the lack of text-to-image experimentation. While the engineering is impressive and the approach is novel, it builds upon existing research in random-order modeling and parallel decoding, making its novelty incremental and the impact substantial but not revolutionary. It's an important step forward, but more advancements are needed to significantly challenge the dominance of diffusion models.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Radar: Fast Long-Context Decoding for Any Transformer
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10571v1)
- **Authors**: Yongchang Hao, Mengyao Zhai, Hossein Hajimirsadeghi, Sepidehsadat Hosseini, Frederick Tung
- **Abstract**: Transformer models have demonstrated exceptional performance across a wide range of applications. Though forming the foundation of Transformer models, the dot-product attention does not scale well to long-context data since its time requirement grows quadratically with context length. In this work, we propose Radar, a training-free approach that accelerates inference by dynamically searching for the most important context tokens. For any pre-trained Transformer, Radar can reduce the decoding time complexity without training or heuristically evicting tokens. Moreover, we provide theoretical justification for our approach, demonstrating that Radar can reliably identify the most important tokens with high probability. We conduct extensive comparisons with the previous methods on a wide range of tasks. The results demonstrate that Radar achieves the state-of-the-art performance across different architectures with reduced time complexity, offering a practical solution for efficient long-context processing of Transformers.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Radar, a training-free approach for accelerating inference in Transformer models, specifically for long-context data.  Radar dynamically identifies important context tokens by grouping them into segments and using random projections to represent each segment. It calculates the importance of each segment when generating a new token, selecting only the tokens in the most relevant segments for attention calculation. The paper provides theoretical justification, showing that Radar reliably identifies important tokens.  Experimental results across diverse tasks and architectures demonstrate Radar's state-of-the-art performance with reduced time complexity compared to existing methods like StreamingLLM, Landmark Attention, H2O, and SnapKV.  The method reduces the complexity to O(t^1.5) where t is context length.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of dynamically selecting context tokens for attention is not entirely new, as methods like H2O and SnapKV already do this. However, Radar differentiates itself by:

    *   Being *training-free*, which is a significant advantage in terms of ease of use and applicability to any pre-trained Transformer.
    *   Providing a *theoretical justification* for its token selection approach, giving more confidence in its effectiveness compared to heuristic-based methods.
    *   Using *random projections* combined with hierarchical structure, offering a specific and efficient mechanism for approximating attention and segment importance.

*   **Significance:**

    *   The long-context problem is a major bottleneck in applying Transformers to many real-world tasks. Radar addresses this by offering a practical solution that accelerates inference without requiring retraining.
    *   The demonstrated state-of-the-art performance across various tasks and models is compelling. The empirical results clearly show Radar's benefits in terms of both speed and quality.
    *   The versatility of Radar (being training-free and applicable to different architectures) makes it a potentially impactful contribution to the field. This removes barriers to adoption and allows existing models to be used effectively on longer contexts.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Strong theoretical underpinnings providing guarantees about the effectiveness of token selection.
    *   Comprehensive experimental evaluation comparing Radar to a range of strong baselines across diverse tasks and architectures.
    *   Clear and well-written paper that explains the method and its benefits.
    *   Detailed time complexity analysis, justifying the efficiency of Radar.
    * The open-source code allows for reproducibility and further experimentation.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The O(t^1.5) complexity is still higher than the ideal O(t) complexity achieved by kernelization methods. While Radar is training-free, kernelization methods, if successfully trained, might offer even better scalability. However, Radar circumvents the increasingly infeasible training for the approximation in linear kernels.
    *   Although the paper shows versatility, it's important to acknowledge that further performance improvements could be achieved by fine-tuning on specific tasks. But that would remove the zero-shot advantage of Radar.
    *   The reliance on random projections introduces a hyperparameter (the projection dimension 'n') that needs to be tuned. Although the paper explores its impact, finding the optimal value might require some experimentation for different datasets and models.
    *   The memory footprint, although acknowledged, is a potential concern for very long contexts.

*   **Potential Influence:** Radar's ease of use and strong performance characteristics make it likely to be adopted by researchers and practitioners working with long-context Transformers. It can also serve as a foundation for future research on more efficient attention mechanisms.

**Overall:**

Radar presents a valuable and well-supported solution to the long-context inference problem in Transformers. It offers a compelling combination of theoretical justification, practical efficiency, and broad applicability. While there are some limitations, its strengths outweigh its weaknesses.

### Unveiling the Mathematical Reasoning in DeepSeek Models: A Comparative Study of Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10573v1)
- **Authors**: Afrar Jahin, Arif Hassan Zidan, Yu Bao, Shizhe Liang, Tianming Liu, Wei Zhang
- **Abstract**: With the rapid evolution of Artificial Intelligence (AI), Large Language Models (LLMs) have reshaped the frontiers of various fields, spanning healthcare, public health, engineering, science, agriculture, education, arts, humanities, and mathematical reasoning. Among these advancements, DeepSeek models have emerged as noteworthy contenders, demonstrating promising capabilities that set them apart from their peers. While previous studies have conducted comparative analyses of LLMs, few have delivered a comprehensive evaluation of mathematical reasoning across a broad spectrum of LLMs. In this work, we aim to bridge this gap by conducting an in-depth comparative study, focusing on the strengths and limitations of DeepSeek models in relation to their leading counterparts. In particular, our study systematically evaluates the mathematical reasoning performance of two DeepSeek models alongside five prominent LLMs across three independent benchmark datasets. The findings reveal several key insights: 1). DeepSeek-R1 consistently achieved the highest accuracy on two of the three datasets, demonstrating strong mathematical reasoning capabilities. 2). The distilled variant of LLMs significantly underperformed compared to its peers, highlighting potential drawbacks in using distillation techniques. 3). In terms of response time, Gemini 2.0 Flash demonstrated the fastest processing speed, outperforming other models in efficiency, which is a crucial factor for real-time applications. Beyond these quantitative assessments, we delve into how architecture, training, and optimization impact LLMs' mathematical reasoning. Moreover, our study goes beyond mere performance comparison by identifying key areas for future advancements in LLM-driven mathematical reasoning. This research enhances our understanding of LLMs' mathematical reasoning and lays the groundwork for future advancements
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents a comparative study evaluating the mathematical reasoning capabilities of DeepSeek models against other prominent Large Language Models (LLMs) like GPT-4o, Gemini 2.0 Flash, and various OpenAI models.  The authors assess the LLMs across three independent benchmark datasets (MATH, GSM8K, and MMLU) using accuracy as the primary metric.  The study reveals that DeepSeek-R1 performs competitively, even outperforming others on some datasets, while distilled variants of LLMs (specifically DeepSeek-1.5B and a distilled OpenAI model) exhibit significantly reduced performance, suggesting a potential downside to distillation techniques for mathematical reasoning. The paper also analyzes response times, finding Gemini 2.0 Flash to be the fastest.  Beyond quantitative results, the paper discusses the architectural and training differences (e.g., the use of GRPO in DeepSeek) and their impact on the mathematical reasoning abilities of these models. The authors highlight the trade-offs between model size, reasoning capability, and computational efficiency.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive Comparison:** The paper offers a direct and reasonably broad comparison of multiple state-of-the-art LLMs on several established mathematical reasoning datasets.
    *   **Focus on DeepSeek:**  The targeted evaluation of DeepSeek models provides valuable insights, given its relative novelty compared to more extensively studied LLMs.
    *   **Analysis Beyond Performance:** The discussion of architectural choices and training paradigms, especially the impact of GRPO in DeepSeek, adds value beyond mere performance ranking.
    *   **Identifies Trade-offs:** The paper appropriately emphasizes the trade-offs between model size/complexity, reasoning ability, and computational efficiency, recognizing that the "best" model depends on the specific application requirements.
    *   **Acknowledges Limitations:** The authors readily admit limitations regarding the scope of LLMs and datasets used.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Incremental Novelty:**  While the comparative study is valuable, the individual components lack substantial novelty.  Comparing LLMs on standard benchmarks is a common practice. The observation that distillation hurts performance in complex tasks is also not entirely new.
    *   **Limited Qualitative Analysis:** While the paper offers a few examples of model reasoning, a deeper qualitative error analysis could reveal more nuanced insights into the strengths and weaknesses of each model.
    *   **Generalizations without Detailed Justification:** Some claims regarding the efficacy of GRPO or CoT are presented with limited supporting evidence beyond the observed performance on the benchmarks.  A more thorough investigation into the specific error types of each model could strengthen these claims.
    *   **API Access Bias**: Using API access introduces uncontrollable elements like server load that may affect response time, which could introduce bias in evaluation.

*   **Significance:**

    *   The paper contributes to the growing body of literature evaluating LLMs' capabilities, particularly in a critical area like mathematical reasoning.
    *   The targeted evaluation of DeepSeek helps position it relative to established models, informing researchers and practitioners.
    *   The findings regarding the impact of distillation serve as a reminder to carefully consider the trade-offs when using model compression techniques, especially for complex reasoning tasks.

*   **Overall Assessment:**

    The paper provides a valuable, albeit incremental, contribution to the field. While the experiments are well-executed and the analysis is sound, the overall novelty is limited. The observation about DeepSeek's competitive performance and the performance degradation of distilled models are important takeaways, but they build upon existing knowledge rather than representing a breakthrough. More in-depth qualitative analysis would have significantly increased the paper's value.

**Score: 6**

*Justification:* The score of 6 reflects that the paper is a solid, well-executed study that provides useful insights into the mathematical reasoning capabilities of several LLMs, including the relatively less-studied DeepSeek models. However, the limited novelty and the lack of deeper qualitative analysis prevent it from achieving a higher score. Its primary contribution is providing more data points and a slightly different perspective on a topic that is already being actively investigated by many researchers. The findings are interesting and relevant, but not transformative.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 6/10

### Unlock the Power of Unlabeled Data in Language Driving Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10586v1)
- **Authors**: Chaoqun Wang, Jie Yang, Xiaobin Hong, Ruimao Zhang
- **Abstract**: Recent Vision-based Large Language Models~(VisionLLMs) for autonomous driving have seen rapid advancements. However, such promotion is extremely dependent on large-scale high-quality annotated data, which is costly and labor-intensive. To address this issue, we propose unlocking the value of abundant yet unlabeled data to improve the language-driving model in a semi-supervised learning manner. Specifically, we first introduce a series of template-based prompts to extract scene information, generating questions that create pseudo-answers for the unlabeled data based on a model trained with limited labeled data. Next, we propose a Self-Consistency Refinement method to improve the quality of these pseudo-annotations, which are later used for further training. By utilizing a pre-trained VisionLLM (e.g., InternVL), we build a strong Language Driving Model (LDM) for driving scene question-answering, outperforming previous state-of-the-art methods. Extensive experiments on the DriveLM benchmark show that our approach performs well with just 5% labeled data, achieving competitive performance against models trained with full datasets. In particular, our LDM achieves 44.85% performance with limited labeled data, increasing to 54.27% when using unlabeled data, while models trained with full datasets reach 60.68% on the DriveLM benchmark.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents a semi-supervised learning approach to improve language-driving models (LDMs) by leveraging unlabeled data.  It addresses the challenges of creating high-quality pseudo-labels for complex VQA tasks in autonomous driving. The approach involves: 1) generating diverse questions from template-based prompts applied to unlabeled data, 2) constructing scene graphs to represent object relationships, and 3) refining pseudo-labels through a self-consistency refinement (SCR) method that considers multiple hints extracted from the scene graph. The refined pseudo-labels are then used to further train the LDM in an iterative fashion.  Experiments on the DriveLM benchmark demonstrate that the proposed method, trained with a small amount of labeled data and large amounts of unlabeled data, achieves competitive performance compared to models trained with full labeled datasets.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its specific combination of techniques for semi-supervised learning in the context of language-driving models. While individual components like template-based prompting, scene graphs, and self-consistency refinement exist in the broader literature, their integrated application to improve LDM performance using unlabeled data, particularly in the driving domain, is a contribution. The specific implementation of SCR using graph-based hints to refine pseudo-labels is also noteworthy. The use of template-based prompts to generate questions based on safe driving principles is a good way to ensure that the questions asked are relevant to the autonomous driving task.
*   **Significance:** The work is significant because it addresses the practical problem of reducing the reliance on costly and labor-intensive high-quality labeled data for training LDMs. Unlocking the potential of readily available unlabeled data can significantly accelerate the development and deployment of autonomous driving systems. The gains achieved in performance, particularly when starting with limited labeled data, demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed approach. The ability to achieve performances that are close to the fully supervised model is a significant advantage. The detailed analysis about different types of hints is also very useful.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Statement:** The paper clearly articulates the motivation for leveraging unlabeled data in language-driving models.
    *   **Well-Defined Approach:** The proposed methodology is well-structured and explained in detail.
    *   **Comprehensive Experiments:** The experiments on the DriveLM benchmark provide strong evidence for the effectiveness of the approach. The ablation studies provide insightful analyses of the components within the algorithm.
    *   **Performance Gains:** The achieved performance gains demonstrate the value of the semi-supervised learning paradigm.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on Pre-trained Models:** The approach relies on the availability of a strong pre-trained VisionLLM (e.g., InternVL2). The performance is likely highly dependent on the quality of this pre-trained model.
    *   **Complexity:** The system is complex, involving multiple stages (question generation, scene graph construction, self-consistency refinement). This complexity could make it challenging to implement and optimize.
    *   **Template-Based Prompts:** While using template-based prompts is a good starting point, the effectiveness of the system depends on the prompt design. Furthermore, the templates are manually defined. There may be a better automatic prompt generation algorithm.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a solid contribution to the field by providing a practical and effective method for improving language-driving models using unlabeled data. The performance gains, particularly with limited labeled data, are significant, and the ablation studies provide valuable insights. However, the reliance on pre-trained models and the complexity of the system are limitations. While the system achieves strong results, it's an incremental improvement with respect to other approaches in semi-supervised and self-supervised learning applied to other fields.

### Long Context Tuning for Video Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10589v1)
- **Authors**: Yuwei Guo, Ceyuan Yang, Ziyan Yang, Zhibei Ma, Zhijie Lin, Zhenheng Yang, Dahua Lin, Lu Jiang
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in video generation can produce realistic, minute-long single-shot videos with scalable diffusion transformers. However, real-world narrative videos require multi-shot scenes with visual and dynamic consistency across shots. In this work, we introduce Long Context Tuning (LCT), a training paradigm that expands the context window of pre-trained single-shot video diffusion models to learn scene-level consistency directly from data. Our method expands full attention mechanisms from individual shots to encompass all shots within a scene, incorporating interleaved 3D position embedding and an asynchronous noise strategy, enabling both joint and auto-regressive shot generation without additional parameters. Models with bidirectional attention after LCT can further be fine-tuned with context-causal attention, facilitating auto-regressive generation with efficient KV-cache. Experiments demonstrate single-shot models after LCT can produce coherent multi-shot scenes and exhibit emerging capabilities, including compositional generation and interactive shot extension, paving the way for more practical visual content creation. See https://guoyww.github.io/projects/long-context-video/ for more details.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Long Context Tuning for Video Generation":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Long Context Tuning (LCT), a novel training paradigm designed to enhance the scene-level consistency of video generation models. LCT builds upon pre-trained single-shot video diffusion models by expanding their context window to encompass multiple shots within a scene. The core components include adapting the full attention mechanism, using interleaved 3D Rotary Positional Embeddings (RoPE), and employing an asynchronous noise strategy.  The method enables both joint and auto-regressive shot generation without requiring additional parameters.  Experiments demonstrate that models fine-tuned with LCT can generate coherent multi-shot scenes, exhibit emerging capabilities like compositional generation and interactive shot extension. A further fine-tuning step using context-causal attention can be used to reduce computational overhead when generating video.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper introduces several novel components, especially the interleaved 3D RoPE, the asynchronous noise strategy, and a pipeline for long context fine tuning. While some elements build upon existing techniques (e.g., diffusion transformers, RoPE), the specific combination and application to multi-shot video generation represents a notable advancement.

*   **Significance:** The significance lies in bridging the gap between single-shot video generation and the more complex demands of scene-level video production. Enabling visual and temporal consistency across multiple shots opens up avenues for more realistic and narrative-driven video content creation. The paper demonstrates this potential with emerging capabilities beyond the base model, like compositional generation and interactive editing.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper effectively articulates the limitations of current single-shot video generation and the need for scene-level consistency.
    *   **Well-Defined Method:** LCT is presented with clear architectural and algorithmic details.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper provides both qualitative and quantitative results, with ablation studies to demonstrate the contribution of different components. They also provide an important user study.
    *   **Emerging Capabilities:** The demonstration of emergent capabilities beyond the training data (e.g., compositional generation) showcases the generalization potential of LCT.
    * The integration of scene level video generation with long context fine tuning as well as a causal attention framework to reduce computational overheard is a novel contribution to this area.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on Gemini-1.5 for Data Curation:** The reliance on Gemini-1.5 for creating scene-level descriptions could introduce biases or inaccuracies in the training data. The quality and consistency of Gemini's output directly impact the learned relationships, and the paper could benefit from a more thorough discussion of potential limitations.
    *   **Qualitative Assessment:** The qualitative evaluations are subject to interpretation. While the examples are compelling, a more rigorous analysis (e.g., blind studies) could further strengthen the findings.
    *   **Complexity**: The approach is necessarily complex. A more detailed discussion of the computational requirements and scalability of the training and inference process would be helpful.
    *   **Limited Scope**: The paper's experiments, while comprehensive, could benefit from testing LCT on a broader range of video datasets and production styles. Furthermore, the "story composition" experiments could be significantly expanded.
    * The description of how scene boundaries and shot cuts are detected could use additional detail.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in video generation by shifting the focus from isolated single-shot synthesis to more holistic, scene-aware models. The modularity of the approach (i.e., it builds on existing single-shot models) makes it more easily adoptable by the community. However, the reliance on third-party models for data curation poses challenges for reproducibility.
*   The discussion of the limitations related to data generation, specifically mentioning biases stemming from relying on third party models would be particularly helpful.

*   Score: 7

    **Justification:**
    A score of 7 is justified because the paper makes significant contributions to the field of video generation by introducing a novel and effective approach for achieving scene-level consistency. However, it's not groundbreaking enough to warrant a higher score due to its reliance on external data generation tools (which creates some black box concerns), some areas where the analysis of the results and the experimental scope could be expanded to provide additional understanding of this architecture.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### CameraCtrl II: Dynamic Scene Exploration via Camera-controlled Video Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10592v1)
- **Authors**: Hao He, Ceyuan Yang, Shanchuan Lin, Yinghao Xu, Meng Wei, Liangke Gui, Qi Zhao, Gordon Wetzstein, Lu Jiang, Hongsheng Li
- **Abstract**: This paper introduces CameraCtrl II, a framework that enables large-scale dynamic scene exploration through a camera-controlled video diffusion model. Previous camera-conditioned video generative models suffer from diminished video dynamics and limited range of viewpoints when generating videos with large camera movement. We take an approach that progressively expands the generation of dynamic scenes -- first enhancing dynamic content within individual video clip, then extending this capability to create seamless explorations across broad viewpoint ranges. Specifically, we construct a dataset featuring a large degree of dynamics with camera parameter annotations for training while designing a lightweight camera injection module and training scheme to preserve dynamics of the pretrained models. Building on these improved single-clip techniques, we enable extended scene exploration by allowing users to iteratively specify camera trajectories for generating coherent video sequences. Experiments across diverse scenarios demonstrate that CameraCtrl Ii enables camera-controlled dynamic scene synthesis with substantially wider spatial exploration than previous approaches.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "CameraCtrl II: Dynamic Scene Exploration via Camera-controlled Video Diffusion Models" introduces an improved framework for generating video sequences with user-specified camera movements, building on the earlier CameraCtrl work. The key improvements address limitations in previous camera-conditioned video generation models: diminished video dynamics and restricted viewpoint ranges. CameraCtrl II enhances video dynamics within individual clips by training on a new dataset curated from real-world dynamic videos and uses a lightweight camera injection module to the video diffusion model. This framework also extends scene exploration by enabling users to iteratively specify camera trajectories, creating coherent video sequences. The authors use Structure-from-Motion to extract camera parameters from real-world videos to build a new training dataset, improving dynamic content generation compared to using solely static scene datasets.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents several novel contributions:

    *   **Dynamic Video Dataset Curation Pipeline:** The pipeline for creating a dynamic video dataset from real-world videos using SfM and specifically addressing issues of scale and trajectory distribution imbalance is a significant step. Using real-world dynamic videos addresses a crucial limitation in the field.
    *   **Lightweight Camera Injection Module:** The simple camera injection approach at the initial layer to preserve video dynamics is a good design choice that distinguishes it from more intrusive methods.
    *   **Clip-wise Autoregressive Generation:** The video extension scheme enables longer, coherent explorations, which is a significant improvement over existing methods.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial problem in camera-controlled video generation: generating dynamic videos and expanding the exploration range. By improving the quality of camera control in diffusion-based generative models, it facilitates richer and more interactive experiences. Addressing the limitations of diminished dynamics and narrow viewpoint ranges is important for practical applications of these models.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Improved Dynamics:** Using a dynamic dataset and a lightweight camera injection method demonstrably enhances the motion and dynamics within generated videos.
    *   **Extended Exploration:** The clip-wise extension allows broader spatial exploration than previous methods.
    *   **Quantitative Evaluation:** The paper provides comprehensive quantitative evaluation metrics, including FVD, motion strength, and camera control accuracy, to demonstrate its superiority over baseline methods.
    *   **Addressing Dataset Bias:** The data curation pipeline tackles the practical problem of trajectory distribution imbalance in real-world data, improving generalization.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on Structure-from-Motion:** The dataset curation still depends on the accuracy of SfM which can struggle in environments with low texture or strong occlusions, creating inaccuracies in the camera parameters. While scale calibration is addressed, limitations on SfM accuracy propagate to the quality of the camera control.
    *   **Computational Cost:** Generating high-resolution and longer dynamic videos using diffusion models is still computationally expensive, despite model distillation efforts.
    *   **Qualitative Failures:** As discussed, the model can generate physically implausible scenes in situations where geometry conflicts with the user-defined camera trajectory, indicating potential limitations with scene understanding.

*   **Impact:** CameraCtrl II has the potential to advance the field of controllable video generation, enabling more realistic and interactive virtual environments. The novel dataset creation method and the design choices in the camera injection and video extension approaches can influence future research in this area.

**Justification:**

While CameraCtrl II presents solid improvements over existing techniques and its novel dataset curation approach is promising, it is not without limitations. The reliance on SfM for camera pose estimation and the existing failure cases indicates opportunities for improvement in scene understanding capabilities. The incremental improvement and reliance of existing techniques gives a good, but not exceptional contribution.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### TruthPrInt: Mitigating LVLM Object Hallucination Via Latent Truthful-Guided Pre-Intervention
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10602v1)
- **Authors**: Jinhao Duan, Fei Kong, Hao Cheng, James Diffenderfer, Bhavya Kailkhura, Lichao Sun, Xiaofeng Zhu, Xiaoshuang Shi, Kaidi Xu
- **Abstract**: Object Hallucination (OH) has been acknowledged as one of the major trustworthy challenges in Large Vision-Language Models (LVLMs). Recent advancements in Large Language Models (LLMs) indicate that internal states, such as hidden states, encode the "overall truthfulness" of generated responses. However, it remains under-explored how internal states in LVLMs function and whether they could serve as "per-token" hallucination indicators, which is essential for mitigating OH. In this paper, we first conduct an in-depth exploration of LVLM internal states in relation to OH issues and discover that (1) LVLM internal states are high-specificity per-token indicators of hallucination behaviors. Moreover, (2) different LVLMs encode universal patterns of hallucinations in common latent subspaces, indicating that there exist "generic truthful directions" shared by various LVLMs. Based on these discoveries, we propose Truthful-Guided Pre-Intervention (TruthPrInt) that first learns the truthful direction of LVLM decoding and then applies truthful-guided inference-time intervention during LVLM decoding. We further propose ComnHallu to enhance both cross-LVLM and cross-data hallucination detection transferability by constructing and aligning hallucination latent subspaces. We evaluate TruthPrInt in extensive experimental settings, including in-domain and out-of-domain scenarios, over popular LVLMs and OH benchmarks. Experimental results indicate that TruthPrInt significantly outperforms state-of-the-art methods. Codes will be available at https://github.com/jinhaoduan/TruthPrInt.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "TruthPrInt: Mitigating LVLM Object Hallucination Via Latent Truthful-Guided Pre-Intervention":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the significant issue of object hallucination (OH) in Large Vision-Language Models (LVLMs).  It investigates whether the internal states of LVLMs, specifically hidden states, can serve as reliable indicators of per-token hallucination behavior.  The authors find that internal states are indeed high-specificity indicators and that different LVLMs share common latent subspaces related to hallucinations, implying "generic truthful directions."  Based on these findings, they propose TruthPrInt, a two-stage framework: 1) Learning the "truthful direction" in the latent space; and 2) applying truthful-guided interventions during LVLM decoding.  They also propose ComnHallu, a method for aligning hallucination subspaces to enhance cross-LVLM and cross-data transferability for hallucination detection.  The method is evaluated across various LVLMs and OH benchmarks, demonstrating significant performance improvements over state-of-the-art approaches in both in-domain and out-of-domain scenarios.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper has several novel aspects. The key contribution lies in explicitly linking the internal states of LVLMs to per-token hallucination behavior. Prior works primarily focused on statistical features or uncertainty quantification at a more global level.  The identification of shared hallucination subspaces across different LVLMs is also a novel and insightful finding. The two-stage approach of TruthPrInt, with pre-intervention based on identifying hallucinated tokens in the latent space, presents a new avenue for mitigating OH. ComnHallu also offers a fresh perspective on improving the transferability of hallucination detection.

*   **Significance:** Addressing OH is a crucial step toward building trustworthy LVLMs.  The proposed method demonstrates a significant advancement in mitigating OH across a variety of models and datasets. The high-specificity aspect of their hallucination detector is particularly valuable, as it minimizes false alarms. The improved transferability through ComnHallu makes the method more practical for real-world applications where OOD scenarios are common. The paper directly tackles a core problem limiting the usefulness of LVLMs and offers a practical, well-evaluated solution.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Rigorous Analysis:** The paper presents a thorough analysis of the internal states of LVLMs. The creation of per-token hallucination datasets and the subsequent training of detectors is well-executed.
    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** The experimental results are compelling, consistently demonstrating the superiority of TruthPrInt over existing methods.  The evaluation includes a range of models, benchmarks, and domain transfer scenarios.
    *   **Clear Problem Definition and Solution:**  The paper clearly articulates the problem of OH, analyzes its causes within LVLMs, and proposes a coherent and effective solution.
    *   **Practicality:** The method is designed to be practical, addressing the issue of domain shift and minimizing computational overhead.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Complexity:** The framework, while effective, introduces additional complexity to the LVLM decoding process. The intervention stage adds computational overhead, though the authors argue it is manageable.
    *   **Dependence on Hallucination Detector:** The performance of TruthPrInt heavily depends on the accuracy and robustness of the hallucination detector. While the paper demonstrates good performance, the detector may still be vulnerable to certain types of hallucinations or adversarial attacks. Further research to harden the detection component would be beneficial.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to significantly influence future research on OH mitigation in LVLMs.  The findings on internal states and shared hallucination subspaces can inspire new approaches to model understanding and intervention. TruthPrInt provides a practical framework that can be extended and adapted for different LVLM architectures and tasks.

**Overall Assessment:**

The paper makes a substantial contribution to the field of trustworthy vision-language modeling. It provides a novel and effective approach to mitigate object hallucination, supported by rigorous analysis and strong empirical results. While the method introduces some complexity, the benefits outweigh the costs, and the potential influence of the paper is significant.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale:** The paper is well-written, presents novel findings, and demonstrates significant improvements over existing methods in mitigating a crucial problem for LVLMs. The key innovation is the link between internal states of LVLMs to per-token hallucination detection. While there are minor limitations regarding complexity and reliance on the hallucination detector, the overall impact is high, justifying a score of 8.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### MuDG: Taming Multi-modal Diffusion with Gaussian Splatting for Urban Scene Reconstruction
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10604v1)
- **Authors**: Yingshuang Zou, Yikang Ding, Chuanrui Zhang, Jiazhe Guo, Bohan Li, Xiaoyang Lyu, Feiyang Tan, Xiaojuan Qi, Haoqian Wang
- **Abstract**: Recent breakthroughs in radiance fields have significantly advanced 3D scene reconstruction and novel view synthesis (NVS) in autonomous driving. Nevertheless, critical limitations persist: reconstruction-based methods exhibit substantial performance deterioration under significant viewpoint deviations from training trajectories, while generation-based techniques struggle with temporal coherence and precise scene controllability. To overcome these challenges, we present MuDG, an innovative framework that integrates Multi-modal Diffusion model with Gaussian Splatting (GS) for Urban Scene Reconstruction. MuDG leverages aggregated LiDAR point clouds with RGB and geometric priors to condition a multi-modal video diffusion model, synthesizing photorealistic RGB, depth, and semantic outputs for novel viewpoints. This synthesis pipeline enables feed-forward NVS without computationally intensive per-scene optimization, providing comprehensive supervision signals to refine 3DGS representations for rendering robustness enhancement under extreme viewpoint changes. Experiments on the Open Waymo Dataset demonstrate that MuDG outperforms existing methods in both reconstruction and synthesis quality.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the MuDG paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "MuDG: Taming Multi-modal Diffusion with Gaussian Splatting for Urban Scene Reconstruction" presents a novel framework that integrates a multi-modal diffusion model with 3D Gaussian Splatting (GS) for urban scene reconstruction and novel view synthesis. The core idea is to leverage a diffusion model conditioned on sparse LiDAR point cloud projections to generate RGB, depth, and semantic videos from novel viewpoints. These generated outputs then serve as supervisory signals to train a Gaussian Splatting model, enhancing its robustness and rendering quality, especially when rendering viewpoints that deviate significantly from the original training trajectories. The framework allows controllable scene editing (e.g., object removal) and demonstrates improved performance on the Open Waymo Dataset compared to existing methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:**  The paper's novelty lies primarily in the *combination* of existing techniques (diffusion models and Gaussian Splatting) in a specific way for urban scene reconstruction.  While neither diffusion models nor GS are individually novel, the proposed integration for improved NVS, particularly under significant viewpoint changes, presents a unique contribution. The key is using diffusion models to generate supervisory data that then guides the GS model. This addresses a recognized weakness in GS, which often struggles with extrapolation beyond training viewpoints. Also the use of Multi-modal supervision (RGB, Depth, Semantics) for 3DGS is novel, it is not common that all these information can be fully combined to enhance 3DGS learning.

* **Significance:** The paper addresses a significant challenge in autonomous driving: creating robust and controllable novel view synthesis for complex urban environments. Reconstruction-based methods often degrade with viewpoint shifts, while purely generative approaches lack temporal coherence and control. MuDG attempts to bridge this gap.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Effective Integration:**  The framework demonstrates a clear and well-reasoned pipeline where the diffusion model's generative power enhances the GS model's rendering capabilities.  The use of multi-modal outputs from the diffusion model (RGB, depth, semantics) is well-motivated and provides a richer supervisory signal than using just RGB images.
    * **Controllability:** The approach shows controllability, enabling scene editing functionalities that are valuable for data augmentation and simulation. The ability to remove objects, modify backgrounds, etc., adds value beyond simple NVS.
    * **Performance:** The experimental results on the Open Waymo Dataset show improvement over existing methods, suggesting the effectiveness of the proposed approach. The ablation studies provide insights into the contribution of different components (control signals, multi-modal supervision).
    * **Clear Presentation:** The paper is well-written and clearly explains the proposed method and its advantages. The figures and tables are informative and support the claims made.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Reliance on Existing Techniques:** The method relies heavily on existing diffusion models and GS.  While the integration is novel, the individual components lack substantial innovation. This limits the originality and scope of impact to the field.
    * **Computational Cost:**  Diffusion models are known to be computationally expensive.  The paper doesn't thoroughly address the computational implications of using MuDG, particularly regarding the training and inference time compared to other approaches. This is a relevant concern given the real-time requirements of autonomous driving applications.
    * **Pseudo Ground Truth:**  The method uses pseudo-ground truth for depth and semantics. The quality of these ground truths directly affects the final result. Although they mention this can be improved, further discussion on its impact is lacking.
    * **Failure Cases:**  The paper acknowledges that the 3DGS module fails on two dynamic scenes due to rare initialization of Gaussian points and the metrics for 3DGS are calculated based on the remaining scenes. A deeper dive into the reason is necessary to ensure the generalization of the approach.

* **Impact and Influence:** MuDG has the potential to influence future research in urban scene reconstruction and novel view synthesis. The integration strategy could inspire other researchers to combine generative and reconstruction-based methods. However, the dependence on computationally intensive diffusion models might limit its adoption in real-time applications unless significant optimizations are achieved. Also, the pseudo ground truth needs some work on to be production ready.

**Justification of Score:**

Considering the novelty, significance, strengths, and weaknesses outlined above, the paper demonstrates a solid, innovative approach to urban scene reconstruction and NVS. It is a well-written and executed work, which makes a meaningful contribution to the domain.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### CoSTA$\ast$: Cost-Sensitive Toolpath Agent for Multi-turn Image Editing
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10613v1)
- **Authors**: Advait Gupta, NandaKiran Velaga, Dang Nguyen, Tianyi Zhou
- **Abstract**: Text-to-image models like stable diffusion and DALLE-3 still struggle with multi-turn image editing. We decompose such a task as an agentic workflow (path) of tool use that addresses a sequence of subtasks by AI tools of varying costs. Conventional search algorithms require expensive exploration to find tool paths. While large language models (LLMs) possess prior knowledge of subtask planning, they may lack accurate estimations of capabilities and costs of tools to determine which to apply in each subtask. Can we combine the strengths of both LLMs and graph search to find cost-efficient tool paths? We propose a three-stage approach "CoSTA*" that leverages LLMs to create a subtask tree, which helps prune a graph of AI tools for the given task, and then conducts A* search on the small subgraph to find a tool path. To better balance the total cost and quality, CoSTA* combines both metrics of each tool on every subtask to guide the A* search. Each subtask's output is then evaluated by a vision-language model (VLM), where a failure will trigger an update of the tool's cost and quality on the subtask. Hence, the A* search can recover from failures quickly to explore other paths. Moreover, CoSTA* can automatically switch between modalities across subtasks for a better cost-quality trade-off. We build a novel benchmark of challenging multi-turn image editing, on which CoSTA* outperforms state-of-the-art image-editing models or agents in terms of both cost and quality, and performs versatile trade-offs upon user preference.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces COSTA*, a cost-sensitive toolpath agent for multi-turn image editing. It addresses the limitations of existing methods, which either rely solely on large language models (LLMs) for planning (potentially leading to suboptimal paths) or expensive graph search techniques (e.g., A* or MCTS) that require extensive exploration. COSTA* combines the strengths of both approaches using a hierarchical planning strategy. First, an LLM generates a subtask tree, pruning the search space.  Then, an A* search is performed on a tool dependency graph (TDG) spanned by the subtask tree to find a cost-efficient toolpath.  COSTA* incorporates prior knowledge of tool capabilities and costs, and dynamically updates the actual execution cost and quality during exploration. This allows users to control the trade-off between quality and cost via a trade-off coefficient. The paper presents a novel benchmark for multi-turn image editing and demonstrates that COSTA* outperforms state-of-the-art methods in terms of both quality and cost. It also includes ablation studies highlighting the importance of real-time feedback and multimodality support.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The core idea of combining LLM-based high-level task decomposition with A*-search based, cost-sensitive tool selection is a significant step forward. While both LLMs and A* are established techniques, the specific way they are integrated to solve the multi-turn image editing problem is relatively novel. COSTA* also stands out through its adaptive strategy, which incorporates real-time feedback for further accuracy and task execution quality, a feature not prominently found in other recent models. The adaptive tuning through the α parameter to emphasize quality or cost is also a valuable contribution.

* **Significance:** Multi-turn image editing is a challenging and practically important task. The paper's ability to outperform existing methods on a newly curated benchmark demonstrates practical significance. The approach is potentially valuable for tasks beyond image editing, such as robotics or any scenario requiring sequential action planning. Also, the novel benchmark introduced in the paper will be valuable for evaluating current and future research efforts in the field.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Effective Hybrid Approach:** The combination of LLM and A* effectively addresses the weaknesses of each approach individually.
    * **Cost-Sensitivity:** Allowing users to trade-off quality and cost is crucial for real-world applications.
    * **Dynamic Adaptation:** The feedback mechanism enables robust path correction during task execution, improving resilience to unexpected outcomes.
    * **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The benchmark, ablation studies, and qualitative results provide strong evidence of the method's effectiveness. The comparison with strong baselines further strengthens the contribution.
    * **Well-Written and Clear:** The paper is easy to follow, and its method is clearly explained with supporting figures and diagrams.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Dependency on LLMs & Pre-trained Models:** Like many contemporary methods, COSTA* relies heavily on the performance of the underlying LLM and pre-trained models. While the authors mitigate this through feedback and exploration, the base capabilities of these models are fundamental.
    * **Complexity:** The system involves multiple components and requires careful tuning.
    * **Limited Real-World Generalization (Potential):** While the curated dataset attempts to simulate real-world complexities, the model needs additional testing in more varied scenarios to truly establish its robustness.
    * **Heuristic Initialization:** The dependency on pre-calculated heuristic estimates could potentially limit the tool path's generalizability or optimality.

* **Potential Influence:** COSTA* establishes a new paradigm for image editing agents. Its performance is impressive. This work demonstrates the effectiveness of combining LLMs with search algorithms, which is likely to inspire further research in this area. The framework provides a general approach that researchers in related fields could adopt.  The benchmarking dataset is also a valuable resource for the community.

**Justification for Score:**

I am assigning a score of 8. The paper presents a novel and effective approach to a challenging problem with strong results and a valuable new benchmark. While it relies on existing models and has a degree of complexity, the integration is novel and addresses significant limitations in the existing literature. Also, the introduction of a cost-quality trade-off adds considerable practical value. Given its potential impact and the quality of the research, a score of 8 is well-justified.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### R1-Onevision: Advancing Generalized Multimodal Reasoning through Cross-Modal Formalization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10615v1)
- **Authors**: Yi Yang, Xiaoxuan He, Hongkun Pan, Xiyan Jiang, Yan Deng, Xingtao Yang, Haoyu Lu, Dacheng Yin, Fengyun Rao, Minfeng Zhu, Bo Zhang, Wei Chen
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models have demonstrated remarkable reasoning capability in complex textual tasks. However, multimodal reasoning, which requires integrating visual and textual information, remains a significant challenge. Existing visual-language models often struggle to effectively analyze and reason visual content, resulting in suboptimal performance on complex reasoning tasks. Moreover, the absence of comprehensive benchmarks hinders the accurate assessment of multimodal reasoning capabilities. In this paper, we introduce R1-Onevision, a multimodal reasoning model designed to bridge the gap between visual perception and deep reasoning. To achieve this, we propose a cross-modal reasoning pipeline that transforms images into formal textural representations, enabling precise language-based reasoning. Leveraging this pipeline, we construct the R1-Onevision dataset which provides detailed, step-by-step multimodal reasoning annotations across diverse domains. We further develop the R1-Onevision model through supervised fine-tuning and reinforcement learning to cultivate advanced reasoning and robust generalization abilities. To comprehensively evaluate multimodal reasoning performance across different grades, we introduce R1-Onevision-Bench, a benchmark aligned with human educational stages, covering exams from junior high school to university and beyond. Experimental results show that R1-Onevision achieves state-of-the-art performance, outperforming models such as GPT-4o and Qwen2.5-VL on multiple challenging multimodal reasoning benchmarks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces R1-Onevision, a multimodal reasoning model designed to improve visual perception and deep reasoning capabilities in visual-language tasks. The core innovation lies in a cross-modal reasoning pipeline that transforms images into formal textural representations, enabling more precise language-based reasoning over visual content. The authors also construct the R1-Onevision dataset, offering detailed, step-by-step multimodal reasoning annotations across diverse domains, and develop the R1-Onevision model through supervised fine-tuning and reinforcement learning.  A new benchmark, R1-Onevision-Bench, is presented to comprehensively evaluate multimodal reasoning performance, aligned with human educational stages from junior high to university level. The experimental results demonstrate R1-Onevision achieves state-of-the-art performance, outperforming models like GPT-4o and Qwen2.5-VL on challenging multimodal reasoning benchmarks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Cross-Modal Formalization:** The key idea of translating images into formal textural representations is novel and promising. This addresses a core weakness in existing models that struggle to bridge the gap between visual perception and symbolic reasoning. This could lead to more reliable and explainable reasoning processes.
    *   **Comprehensive Dataset and Benchmark:** The R1-Onevision dataset and R1-Onevision-Bench address a significant gap in multimodal reasoning benchmarks.  By focusing on diverse domains and educational levels, the benchmark offers a more realistic and challenging evaluation of model capabilities. This could drive further improvements in model design. The detailed step-by-step annotations within the dataset offer valuable training signals.
    *   **Strong Performance:**  The experimental results show that R1-Onevision achieves state-of-the-art performance, suggesting the effectiveness of the proposed approach.
    *   **Detailed analysis:** The ablation study provides important insights into the influence of the SFT and RL phases, which allow the team to improve the model's performance.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on GPT-40:** A critical component of the data generation pipeline relies on GPT-40 and other large language models. While convenient, this introduces a potential bias in the generated data and annotations based on GPT-4o's limitations and biases.
    *   **Scalability concerns**: Given that the final goal is to help model adapt the performance to multiple areas, the method appears not so scalable due to the limited source of images and data.
    *   **Generalizability of Formalization:** The approach relies on converting images to structured textual representations. It's unclear how well this strategy will generalize to tasks where the core challenge is *understanding* the raw pixel data directly, without the intermediate symbolic representation. Consider the reliance on data extracted by Deepseek R1 to be able to perform the generation.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    *   The combination of cross-modal formalization, a meticulously constructed dataset, and a tailored training pipeline represents a significant advancement in multimodal reasoning.  The work moves beyond simple image captioning and attempts to truly encode visual information in a way that facilitates deeper reasoning. The approach tackles a genuine problem in current multimodal reasoning techniques. While the reliance on existing LLMs for data generation introduces some caveats, the overall framework is well-structured and shows promise.

*   **Potential Influence:**

    *   The paper could inspire new research directions in multimodal reasoning, especially on structured representations and reasoning. The R1-Onevision dataset and benchmark have the potential to become valuable resources for the community, encouraging the development of more robust and generalizable models.

*   **Justification of Score:**

    The paper addresses a critical problem in multimodal reasoning and provides a novel and well-executed solution. However, the reliance on LLMs for data generation and the limitations on scalability and generalizability somewhat temper the overall impact.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Compositional Subspace Representation Fine-tuning for Adaptive Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10617v1)
- **Authors**: Andy Zhou
- **Abstract**: Adapting large language models to multiple tasks can cause cross-skill interference, where improvements for one skill degrade another. While methods such as LoRA impose orthogonality constraints at the weight level, they do not fully address interference in hidden-state representations. We propose Compositional Subspace Representation Fine-tuning (CS-ReFT), a novel representation-based approach that learns multiple orthonormal subspace transformations, each specializing in a distinct skill, and composes them via a lightweight router. By isolating these subspace edits in the hidden state, rather than weight matrices, CS-ReFT prevents cross-task conflicts more effectively. On the AlpacaEval benchmark, applying CS-ReFT to Llama-2-7B achieves a 93.94% win rate, surpassing GPT-3.5 Turbo (86.30%) while requiring only 0.0098% of model parameters. These findings show that specialized representation edits, composed via a simple router, significantly enhance multi-task instruction following with minimal overhead.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Compositional Subspace Representation Fine-tuning (CS-ReFT), a new method for adapting large language models (LLMs) to multiple tasks. CS-ReFT addresses the problem of cross-task interference, where improving performance on one task degrades performance on another.  It achieves this by learning multiple orthonormal subspace transformations, each specializing in a distinct skill, and composing them via a lightweight router.  These subspace edits are applied directly to the hidden states of the LLM rather than the weight matrices, enabling better isolation of skills and preventing cross-task conflicts.  The authors demonstrate that CS-ReFT applied to Llama-2-7B achieves a high win rate on the AlpacaEval benchmark, surpassing GPT-3.5 Turbo, while requiring only a tiny fraction of model parameters to be trained.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Addresses a critical problem:** Cross-task interference is a significant challenge in multi-task LLM adaptation, and CS-ReFT offers a promising approach to mitigate it.
    *   **Novelty in the representation space:** Moving beyond weight-based modifications (like LoRA) and directly editing hidden state representations with orthonormal constraints is a strong conceptual contribution. This is a key differentiator.
    *   **Parameter efficiency:** The method achieves state-of-the-art performance while requiring very few trainable parameters. This is a practical benefit for deployment.
    *   **Empirical validation:** The AlpacaEval results are impressive, showing that CS-ReFT outperforms both larger models and parameter-efficient baselines.
    *   **Compositional Approach:** The framework introduces dynamic composition via router that allows the model to use appropriate subset of transformation for an input.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Task Definition:** The paper relies on manually-defined tasks for training the subspaces. While functional, automatic task discovery would enhance the method’s applicability.
    *   **Limited Experimental Scope:** The empirical evaluation is primarily focused on AlpacaEval. Testing on a broader range of multi-task benchmarks would strengthen the claims.
    *   **Router Interpretability:** Although router is small and efficient, its interpretability is not assessed. Knowing how it works is valuable.
    *   **Scalability:** The current architecture may not scale well to situations involving a very large number of tasks.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    The core novelty lies in the combination of orthonormal subspace representation editing with a lightweight router for compositional skill adaptation. While representation editing and orthogonal constraints have been explored before, their combination in CS-ReFT, specifically applied to multi-task learning and with a routing mechanism, is a significant advancement. The method offers a more structured way to prevent cross-task interference compared to generic representation editing techniques. The high win rate on AlpacaEval provides strong evidence of its practical value.

    However, the limited scope of evaluation slightly weakens the impact. Broader benchmark evaluations and further insights on router dynamics would elevate the significance.

*   **Potential Influence:**

    CS-ReFT has the potential to influence the development of more efficient and effective multi-task LLM adaptation methods. Its modular, compositional approach could be particularly relevant for continual learning and lifelong learning scenarios.  The idea of explicitly isolating skills within the hidden state space is likely to inspire further research in this area.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:**

CS-ReFT addresses a vital problem (cross-task interference) with a novel and technically sound approach (orthonormal subspace editing and dynamic routing). The AlpacaEval results are compelling.  The relatively limited experimental scope and lack of interpretability analysis restrain the score. The method shows significant potential, justifying a score of 7.5. Further research and broader evaluations could potentially increase this score.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### DiT-Air: Revisiting the Efficiency of Diffusion Model Architecture Design in Text to Image Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10618v1)
- **Authors**: Chen Chen, Rui Qian, Wenze Hu, Tsu-Jui Fu, Lezhi Li, Bowen Zhang, Alex Schwing, Wei Liu, Yinfei Yang
- **Abstract**: In this work, we empirically study Diffusion Transformers (DiTs) for text-to-image generation, focusing on architectural choices, text-conditioning strategies, and training protocols. We evaluate a range of DiT-based architectures--including PixArt-style and MMDiT variants--and compare them with a standard DiT variant which directly processes concatenated text and noise inputs. Surprisingly, our findings reveal that the performance of standard DiT is comparable with those specialized models, while demonstrating superior parameter-efficiency, especially when scaled up. Leveraging the layer-wise parameter sharing strategy, we achieve a further reduction of 66% in model size compared to an MMDiT architecture, with minimal performance impact. Building on an in-depth analysis of critical components such as text encoders and Variational Auto-Encoders (VAEs), we introduce DiT-Air and DiT-Air-Lite. With supervised and reward fine-tuning, DiT-Air achieves state-of-the-art performance on GenEval and T2I CompBench, while DiT-Air-Lite remains highly competitive, surpassing most existing models despite its compact size.
- **Summary**: The paper "DiT-Air: Revisiting the Efficiency of Diffusion Model Architecture Design in Text to Image Generation" empirically studies Diffusion Transformers (DiTs) for text-to-image generation, focusing on architectural choices, text-conditioning strategies, and training protocols. The authors find that a standard DiT architecture, when enhanced with shared AdaLN parameters and processing concatenated text and noise inputs directly, achieves comparable or superior performance to more specialized models like PixArt-style and MMDiT, especially when scaled up. They further reduce the model size using layer-wise parameter sharing strategies. The resulting models, DiT-Air and DiT-Air-Lite, achieve state-of-the-art performance on GenEval and T2I CompBench, with DiT-Air-Lite remaining competitive despite its compact size. The work includes a thorough analysis of text encoders and VAEs, proposing a refined VAE for better image quality.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper presents a valuable empirical study on DiT architectures for text-to-image generation. The key novelty lies in the finding that a simplified DiT architecture can match or exceed the performance of more complex designs with careful parameter tuning and scaling. The parameter sharing strategies, while not entirely new in the broader context of transformer models, are effectively applied to DiTs to achieve substantial model size reductions with minimal performance impact. The analysis of different text encoders and VAEs, along with the proposed progressive VAE training strategy, contributes to a better understanding of the key components in text-to-image generation pipelines.
