Building upon these insights, we propose a Few-shot Attention Intervention method (FAI) that dynamically analyzes the attention patterns of demonstrations to accurately identify these tokens and subsequently make targeted adjustments to the attention weights to effectively suppress their distracting effect on LLMs. Comprehensive experiments across multiple benchmarks demonstrate consistent improvements over baseline methods, with a remarkable 5.91% improvement on the AQuA dataset, further highlighting the effectiveness of FAI.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Don't Take Things Out of Context: Attention Intervention for Enhancing Chain-of-Thought Reasoning in Large Language Models" addresses the issue of sensitivity to CoT demonstration selection within LLMs. The authors observe that specific tokens within demonstrations can "distract" LLMs, causing them to focus on irrelevant local information leading to incorrect answers. They hypothesize that tokens with certain attention characteristics (low information aggregation) are particularly susceptible to causing this issue. To combat this, they propose Few-shot Attention Intervention (FAI), a method that analyzes attention patterns to identify such distracting tokens and dynamically adjust attention weights to suppress their influence. The paper presents experimental results on multiple reasoning benchmarks, demonstrating consistent performance improvements over baselines when using FAI.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a reasonably novel approach to improving CoT reasoning by dynamically intervening in the attention mechanism of LLMs. While previous work has addressed the sensitivity of CoT to demonstration selection, the specific focus on identifying and suppressing distracting tokens through attention analysis is a valuable contribution. The method of calculating the "aggregation coefficient" to identify tokens that have not undergone significant information aggregation is new.

*   **Significance:** The problem addressed is significant. CoT's sensitivity to demonstration selection hinders its reliability and applicability in real-world scenarios. By improving the robustness of CoT, this paper contributes towards more reliable and predictable LLM reasoning. The observed improvements on benchmarks, particularly the substantial 5.91% increase on AQuA, suggest a practical benefit.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The problem framing is clear and well-motivated with illustrative examples and a detailed analysis of attention salience.
    *   The proposed FAI method is intuitive and based on a plausible hypothesis about how LLMs process CoT demonstrations.
    *   The experimental results demonstrate consistent improvements across multiple benchmarks, lending credence to the effectiveness of the proposed approach.
    *   The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the workings of FAI, revealing that intervening on tokens exhibiting low information aggregation is crucial for its performance.
    *   The paper carefully considers the impact on the positive effect of CoT, with experiments focusing on RAFR (Rate of Answer Following the Rationale)
    *   The analysis of token identified is relevant, showing that the proposed method focused on a relevant set of tokens

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The definition of the threshold *T* relies on an approximation of the mean of the attention, that could be improved with more accurate values
    *   The study could benefit from a more detailed analysis of the types of distracting tokens and the specific mechanisms by which they interfere with reasoning.
    *   While the benchmarks are diverse, further investigation into the method's performance on more complex, open-ended reasoning tasks could be valuable.
    *   The experiments are limited to models with a scale under 70B parameters.
    *   The analysis of the internal mechanism of the distraction effect is limited to attention and salience maps. Further exploration on the activation level and feature extraction is relevant.
    *   The approach could be more broadly applicable.

*   **Potential Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence future research on CoT prompting and LLM interpretability. By demonstrating the importance of attention patterns in CoT reasoning, it could inspire the development of more sophisticated attention-based intervention techniques. The findings may also have practical implications for improving the reliability of LLMs in various applications.

*   **Score Justification:** The paper presents a novel and well-motivated method for improving the robustness of CoT reasoning. The experimental results are promising, and the ablation studies provide valuable insights. While there are some limitations, the paper makes a significant contribution to the field and has the potential to influence future research. Considering both the strengths and weaknesses, a score of 7 is appropriate.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Towards Extreme Pruning of LLMs with Plug-and-Play Mixed Sparsity
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.11164v1)
- **Authors**: Chi Xu, Gefei Zhang, Yantong Zhu, Luca Benini, Guosheng Hu, Yawei Li, Zhihong Zhang
- **Abstract**: N:M structured pruning is essential for large language models (LLMs) because it can remove less important network weights and reduce the memory and computation requirements. Existing pruning methods mainly focus on designing metrics to measure the importance of network components to guide pruning. Apart from the impact of these metrics, we observe that different layers have different sensitivities over the network performance. Thus, we propose an efficient method based on the trace of Fisher Information Matrix (FIM) to quantitatively measure and verify the different sensitivities across layers. Based on this, we propose Mixed Sparsity Pruning (MSP) which uses a pruning-oriented evolutionary algorithm (EA) to determine the optimal sparsity levels for different layers. To guarantee fast convergence and achieve promising performance, we utilize efficient FIM-inspired layer-wise sensitivity to initialize the population of EA. In addition, our MSP can work as a plug-and-play module, ready to be integrated into existing pruning methods. Extensive experiments on LLaMA and LLaMA-2 on language modeling and zero-shot tasks demonstrate our superior performance. In particular, in extreme pruning ratio (e.g. 75%), our method significantly outperforms existing methods in terms of perplexity (PPL) by orders of magnitude (Figure 1).
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel approach to pruning large language models (LLMs) called Mixed Sparsity Pruning (MSP). Unlike traditional pruning methods that apply a uniform sparsity ratio across all layers, MSP leverages the observation that different layers exhibit varying sensitivities to pruning. To quantify this sensitivity, the authors propose using the trace of the Fisher Information Matrix (FIM). Based on the FIM-derived layer sensitivities, MSP employs a pruning-oriented evolutionary algorithm (EA) to determine the optimal sparsity levels for each layer. The method is designed as a plug-and-play module that can be integrated with existing pruning techniques to improve their performance, especially at high sparsity ratios. Experimental results on LLaMA and LLaMA-2 demonstrate that MSP significantly outperforms existing methods in terms of perplexity and zero-shot task accuracy, particularly at high sparsity levels (e.g., 75%).

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of layer-wise adaptive sparsity based on FIM sensitivity is a valuable contribution.  Most existing pruning methods operate on uniform sparsity assumptions, which are demonstrably suboptimal. The paper clearly articulates this limitation and proposes a principled way to address it.
*   **Technical Soundness:** The use of the FIM trace to estimate layer sensitivity provides a computationally efficient alternative to Hessian-based methods, which are impractical for large models.  The design of the EA is tailored to the pruning problem, with crossover and mutation operators that respect the target sparsity constraints.
*   **Empirical Validation:** The experiments are thorough and well-designed. The paper presents compelling evidence that MSP significantly improves the performance of several SOTA pruning methods across various LLMs, sparsity levels, and evaluation metrics (perplexity, zero-shot accuracy). The ablation studies provide insights into the importance of sensitivity-informed initialization and the effects of evolutionary parameters. The exploration of calibration data robustness is also commendable.
*   **Practicality:** The plug-and-play nature of MSP is a major strength, allowing it to be easily integrated into existing pruning pipelines, enhancing the effectiveness of other techniques without requiring extensive modifications.
*   **Clarity:** The paper is well-written and easy to understand. The motivation for MSP is clearly articulated, and the technical details are presented in a concise and accessible manner. The figures and tables effectively illustrate the key results and findings.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Computational Overhead:** Although the paper emphasizes the efficiency of FIM-based sensitivity measurement, the use of an evolutionary algorithm introduces additional computational costs. The benefits of MSP need to be weighed against this overhead, particularly for resource-constrained settings.  More discussion of the computational demands of the EA would be beneficial.
*   **Limited Theoretical Analysis:** While the paper provides empirical evidence of the effectiveness of MSP, a deeper theoretical analysis of the relationship between FIM sensitivity and optimal sparsity levels would strengthen the work.  A more formal justification of why the FIM trace is a good proxy for layer sensitivity in the context of pruning would be valuable.
*   **Generalization to other Tasks:** The evaluation primarily focuses on language modeling and zero-shot tasks. It would be beneficial to assess the performance of MSP on other downstream tasks, such as fine-tuning or transfer learning, to further demonstrate its generalizability.
*   **Parameter Sensitivity:** The EA has several hyperparameters (mutation rate, population size, number of generations). While the paper includes an ablation study, a more comprehensive analysis of the sensitivity of MSP to these hyperparameters could improve its robustness and ease of use.
*   **Scalability Issues:** The authors used two NVIDIA A100 GPUs. This might not be readily available for others interested in reproducing their work.

**Significance:**

The paper makes a significant contribution to the field of LLM pruning by introducing a novel and effective approach for adaptive layer-wise sparsity. The results demonstrate that MSP can significantly improve the performance of existing pruning methods, enabling more aggressive compression without sacrificing accuracy. The plug-and-play nature of MSP makes it a valuable tool for practitioners seeking to deploy LLMs in resource-constrained environments. The insights into layer sensitivity and optimal sparsity allocation could inspire further research in this area.

**Overall:**

The paper addresses a crucial problem in LLM deployment and offers a compelling solution. The proposed MSP method is technically sound, empirically validated, and practically useful. The minor weaknesses mentioned above do not detract significantly from the overall value of the contribution. The paper has the potential to influence the direction of research in LLM pruning and to facilitate the wider adoption of these models.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale:**

The paper demonstrates strong novelty in its approach to adaptive layer-wise sparsity.  The empirical results convincingly show substantial performance gains with a practical plug-and-play design.  The main detracting factors are the EA's computational overhead and the lack of a more in-depth theoretical justification for the use of the FIM trace, alongside the generalisation of the method to other tasks outside of perplexity and zero-shot. However, the significant performance improvements and the practical nature of the approach warrant a high score, reflecting its potential impact on the field.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### Neurons: Emulating the Human Visual Cortex Improves Fidelity and Interpretability in fMRI-to-Video Reconstruction
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.11167v1)
- **Authors**: Haonan Wang, Qixiang Zhang, Lehan Wang, Xuanqi Huang, Xiaomeng Li
- **Abstract**: Decoding visual stimuli from neural activity is essential for understanding the human brain. While fMRI methods have successfully reconstructed static images, fMRI-to-video reconstruction faces challenges due to the need for capturing spatiotemporal dynamics like motion and scene transitions. Recent approaches have improved semantic and perceptual alignment but struggle to integrate coarse fMRI data with detailed visual features. Inspired by the hierarchical organization of the visual system, we propose NEURONS, a novel framework that decouples learning into four correlated sub-tasks: key object segmentation, concept recognition, scene description, and blurry video reconstruction. This approach simulates the visual cortex's functional specialization, allowing the model to capture diverse video content. In the inference stage, NEURONS generates robust conditioning signals for a pre-trained text-to-video diffusion model to reconstruct the videos. Extensive experiments demonstrate that NEURONS outperforms state-of-the-art baselines, achieving solid improvements in video consistency (26.6%) and semantic-level accuracy (19.1%). Notably, NEURONS shows a strong functional correlation with the visual cortex, highlighting its potential for brain-computer interfaces and clinical applications. Code and model weights will be available at: https://github.com/xmed-lab/NEURONS.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "NEURONS: Emulating the Human Visual Cortex Improves Fidelity and Interpretability in fMRI-to-Video Reconstruction" introduces a novel framework for reconstructing videos from fMRI data. Inspired by the hierarchical structure of the human visual cortex, the framework decouples the learning process into four correlated sub-tasks: key object segmentation, concept recognition, scene description, and blurry video reconstruction.  By simulating the functional specialization of the visual cortex, the model aims to capture diverse video content and generate robust conditioning signals for a pre-trained text-to-video diffusion model. Experiments demonstrate that NEURONS outperforms state-of-the-art baselines in video consistency and semantic-level accuracy and shows a strong functional correlation with the visual cortex.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The key novelty lies in the *explicitly* modeling fMRI-to-video reconstruction as a set of decoupled tasks mirroring the functional organization of the human visual cortex. While previous methods have used deep learning and aligned fMRI data with pre-trained models like CLIP, this paper takes a step further by decomposing the learning process. The progressive learning strategy with dynamically adjusted loss weights is another novel aspect. Also the use of rule-based key object discovery is interesting for identifying the primary object in the videos.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a significant challenge in the field: reconstructing dynamic visual experiences (videos) from fMRI data, which is more complex than static image reconstruction.  The performance improvements over existing methods, particularly in video consistency, are substantial and demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed approach.  The interpretability aspect, linking model components to specific visual cortex regions, is a significant contribution, potentially paving the way for more biologically plausible and useful brain-computer interfaces. The functional consistency with the visual cortex is a strong selling point.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The neuroscientific inspiration and clear mapping of model components to brain regions provide strong justification for the approach.
    *   The experimental results are compelling, with significant improvements over state-of-the-art methods.
    *   The ablation studies provide insights into the contribution of each sub-task and the progressive learning strategy.
    *   The paper is well-written and clearly explains the methodology.
    * Qualitative results, with descriptive comments are used effectively for comparisons.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    * The reliance on external datasets like Kinetics-400 and pre-trained models (CLIP, Stable Diffusion, Qwen, Grounded SAM, GPT-2, AnimateDiff) introduces potential biases and limitations. The results are contingent on the quality of these models. The approach essentially guides a pre-trained T2V model, so the baseline performance of T2V will also play a significant role.
    * While the interpretablity with the visual cortex is an important claim, more robust evaluations could be done. Simply visualizing weights has limitations.
    *  There is limited exploration of the clinical applications, which are mentioned as a potential benefit.
    *  Generalization to other datasets or fMRI recording settings might be limited. Data is only from one fmri-video dataset.
    * Implementation is complex, leveraging several foundation models. This can limit adoption.
    * Although authors highlight that their approach performs better in key object localization, DICE score remains relatively low at 35%.

*   **Potential Impact:** The paper has the potential to significantly influence the field of fMRI decoding and brain-computer interfaces. The interpretable nature of the model could lead to a better understanding of how the brain processes visual information. The improved reconstruction quality could have applications in assistive technologies for individuals with visual impairments and clinical applications.

**Justification for the Score:**

The paper demonstrates significant novelty by explicitly decoupling the visual reconstruction task based on functional organization in visual cortices. The impressive gains made in video quality and semantic representation relative to state of the art provide evidence that this is more than just a conceptual advance. Weakness include a reliance on various foundation model which introduces bias, and limitations of the current approach which focuses mostly on improving the generated video while providing only interpretable weight visualizations of visual cortex region. Overall, the advances are significant given the complexity and difficulty of fmri-to-video construction.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Multi-Stage Generative Upscaler: Reconstructing Football Broadcast Images via Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.11181v1)
- **Authors**: Luca Martini, Daniele Zolezzi, Saverio Iacono, Gianni Viardo Vercelli
- **Abstract**: The reconstruction of low-resolution football broadcast images presents a significant challenge in sports broadcasting, where detailed visuals are essential for analysis and audience engagement. This study introduces a multi-stage generative upscaling framework leveraging Diffusion Models to enhance degraded images, transforming inputs as small as $64 \times 64$ pixels into high-fidelity $1024 \times 1024$ outputs. By integrating an image-to-image pipeline, ControlNet conditioning, and LoRA fine-tuning, our approach surpasses traditional upscaling methods in restoring intricate textures and domain-specific elements such as player details and jersey logos. The custom LoRA is trained on a custom football dataset, ensuring adaptability to sports broadcast needs. Experimental results demonstrate substantial improvements over conventional models, with ControlNet refining fine details and LoRA enhancing task-specific elements. These findings highlight the potential of diffusion-based image reconstruction in sports media, paving the way for future applications in automated video enhancement and real-time sports analytics.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a concise summary, a critical evaluation of novelty and significance, and a score with thorough justification for the provided paper.

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a multi-stage generative upscaling framework based on diffusion models to reconstruct low-resolution football broadcast images into high-fidelity versions. The framework consists of three stages: first, image standardization and initial upscaling using an image-to-image pipeline; second, refinement of finer details using ControlNet; and third, fine-tuning with a custom LoRA model trained on a football-specific dataset to enhance domain-specific elements like player details and jersey logos. The authors demonstrate improved clarity and structural fidelity compared to traditional upscaling methods and show the potential for applications in sports media, automated video enhancement, and real-time sports analytics.

**Critical Evaluation of Novelty and Significance:**

The paper presents a reasonable approach to the problem of reconstructing low-resolution broadcast images, leveraging the power of diffusion models. While each component (diffusion models, ControlNet, LoRA) is not novel *per se*, the specific combination and application to this particular domain (football broadcast image upscaling) have some merits.

**Strengths:**

*   **Application to a Relevant Problem:**  The reconstruction of low-resolution sports broadcast images is a practical problem, particularly given their use in post-match analysis.
*   **Well-Defined Pipeline:** The paper clearly outlines the multi-stage pipeline and explains the rationale for each component.
*   **Use of Established Techniques:**  The authors appropriately employ current state-of-the-art methods, including diffusion models, ControlNet, and LoRA fine-tuning.
*   **Custom LoRA training:** Training a custom LoRA on a dataset specifically for football images is a good approach for domain adaptation.
*   **Good Implementation Details:** The paper provides clear implementation details and discusses optimization strategies, enhancing reproducibility.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Novelty in Methodological Combination:**  The core contribution lies in combining existing techniques, rather than introducing a fundamentally new method. There isn't a significant theoretical advancement or innovative modification of these existing techniques.
*   **Limited Novelty in Domain Application:** The choice of problem (football broadcast image upscaling) is practically valuable. While the domain specific details (faces and textures) are highlighted, these details are not new or significantly different when adapting the established methods.
*   **Lack of Thorough Comparison:** While the authors briefly mention the failure of Real-ESRGAN, a more comprehensive comparison against other established super-resolution techniques (especially those adapted to low-resolution image enhancement) would strengthen the paper. Quantitative metrics (e.g., PSNR, SSIM, LPIPS) are entirely absent, making it difficult to assess the true performance gains. The visual comparisons in Figure 3 are not convincing enough.
*   **Lack of ablation Studies:** The paper lacks thorough ablation studies. For example, an ablation study of different LoRA configurations, impact of each components could strengthen the claim.
*   **Limited discussion on failure cases**: The authors do not delve into the failure cases or limitations of the proposed approach.

**Significance:**

The paper's significance is moderate. It showcases the applicability of diffusion models for a specific real-world problem. However, the lack of substantial novelty in the method and the limited empirical evaluation hinder its potential impact on the broader field of image super-resolution or generative modeling. The contribution is more application-oriented than theoretically driven. It provides a practical recipe for others to follow in similar scenarios.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a useful application of existing techniques to a relevant problem. However, the incremental novelty of the method and the limited evaluation lead to a lower score. It doesn't provide significant new insights or push the boundaries of the field in a significant way. It can be valuable to researchers and engineers working in a similar applications to sports, but the general contributions are limited.

### Palette of Language Models: A Solver for Controlled Text Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.11182v1)
- **Authors**: Zhe Yang, Yi Huang, Yaqin Chen, Xiaoting Wu, Junlan Feng, Chao Deng
- **Abstract**: Recent advancements in large language models have revolutionized text generation with their remarkable capabilities. These models can produce controlled texts that closely adhere to specific requirements when prompted appropriately. However, designing an optimal prompt to control multiple attributes simultaneously can be challenging. A common approach is to linearly combine single-attribute models, but this strategy often overlooks attribute overlaps and can lead to conflicts. Therefore, we propose a novel combination strategy inspired by the Law of Total Probability and Conditional Mutual Information Minimization on generative language models. This method has been adapted for single-attribute control scenario and is termed the Palette of Language Models due to its theoretical linkage between attribute strength and generation style, akin to blending colors on an artist's palette. Moreover, positive correlation and attribute enhancement are advanced as theoretical properties to guide a rational combination strategy design. We conduct experiments on both single control and multiple control settings, and achieve surpassing results.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Palette of Language Models: A Solver for Controlled Text Generation":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel approach for controlled text generation by combining multiple language models, each associated with a specific attribute. Addressing the limitations of linear combination methods (which can overlook attribute overlaps and conflicts), the authors propose a combination strategy inspired by the Law of Total Probability and Conditional Mutual Information Minimization.  This method, termed "Palette of Language Models," aims to dynamically adjust attribute strengths and generation styles, drawing an analogy to blending colors on an artist's palette.  The authors also present theoretical properties (positive correlation and attribute enhancement) to guide the combination strategy.  They conduct experiments in both single and multi-attribute control scenarios, demonstrating improvements over baseline methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Novel Approach:** The core idea of using the Law of Total Probability and Conditional Mutual Information to address attribute overlap in controlled text generation is a significant departure from simpler linear combination techniques. It provides a theoretically grounded framework for blending different language models.
*   **Theoretical Foundation:** The paper provides a relatively rigorous mathematical derivation for their approach. Presenting and proving "positive correlation" and "attribute enhancement" lends credibility to the methodology and helps in understanding how the combination strategy affects generation.
*   **Comprehensive Experiments:** The paper includes experiments covering both single-attribute and multi-attribute control scenarios, demonstrating the versatility of the proposed method. The inclusion of various baselines (Self-Debiasing, FUDGE, PREADD, Linear combination) provides a solid comparative analysis.
*   **Addresses a Real Problem:** Controlled text generation is a vital area, and the paper directly tackles the challenges of combining multiple attributes effectively.  The explicit addressing of attribute conflict and overlap is a practical and valuable contribution.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Complexity:**  The mathematical derivation and explanation of the method can be challenging to follow. This may limit the accessibility of the work to a wider audience.  The explanation of how the parameters are tuned (e.g., variable `t` for complementary event) could be clearer.
*   **Limited Scope of Language Models:** While the authors use Llama2-7b, Pythia-12b and MPT-7b, exploring their framework with more varied LM families (e.g. open-source ones) and parameter sizes would bolster confidence.
*   **Limited discussion on computational cost:** The article does not include an estimation for the computational cost of the method, which is a critical aspect when considering deploying and using this approach in real-world scenarios.
*   **Evaluation Metrics:** The use of toxicity score, sentiment score and perplexity as the main evaluation metrics may not fully capture the nuances and quality of generated text. More sophisticated metrics or human evaluation could enhance the evaluation.
*   **Incremental Improvement:**  While the results show improvements over baselines, the magnitude of these improvements in some cases may be incremental.  A more compelling demonstration of the method's superiority would be beneficial.
*   **Lack of ablation studies**: The absence of ablation experiments makes it challenging to assess the specific contributions of individual components of the proposed method. For instance, evaluating the impact of the Conditional Mutual Information Minimization (CMIM) term separately would provide valuable insights into its effectiveness.

**Novelty and Significance:**

The paper introduces a novel method that brings together the Law of Total Probability and conditional mutual information minimization in order to better facilitate multi-attribute text generation. This combination of concepts in the context of controlled text generation offers a potentially important approach with significant impact on the field.

**Potential Influence:**

The paper is likely to influence future research in controlled text generation, specifically regarding how to combine multiple attributes effectively.  The theoretical framework and experimental results could serve as a basis for further advancements in this area.  The notion of modeling attribute overlap and dynamically adjusting attribute strengths is a valuable insight.

**Score:** 7

**Rationale:** The paper presents a novel, theoretically grounded method for controlled text generation, backed by comprehensive experiments. The method effectively addresses a real-world problem and offers an alternative way of thinking about attribute combination, which warrants a fairly positive score. While there are some weaknesses related to limited diversity in language models used, complexity, scope of evaluation, and incremental gains, the contributions are substantial enough to warrant a score of 7.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Align in Depth: Defending Jailbreak Attacks via Progressive Answer Detoxification
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.11185v1)
- **Authors**: Yingjie Zhang, Tong Liu, Zhe Zhao, Guozhu Meng, Kai Chen
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) are vulnerable to jailbreak attacks, which use crafted prompts to elicit toxic responses. These attacks exploit LLMs' difficulty in dynamically detecting harmful intents during the generation process. Traditional safety alignment methods, often relying on the initial few generation steps, are ineffective due to limited computational budget. This paper proposes DEEPALIGN, a robust defense framework that fine-tunes LLMs to progressively detoxify generated content, significantly improving both the computational budget and effectiveness of mitigating harmful generation. Our approach uses a hybrid loss function operating on hidden states to directly improve LLMs' inherent awareness of toxity during generation. Furthermore, we redefine safe responses by generating semantically relevant answers to harmful queries, thereby increasing robustness against representation-mutation attacks. Evaluations across multiple LLMs demonstrate state-of-the-art defense performance against six different attack types, reducing Attack Success Rates by up to two orders of magnitude compared to previous state-of-the-art defense while preserving utility. This work advances LLM safety by addressing limitations of conventional alignment through dynamic, context-aware mitigation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Align in Depth: Defending Jailbreak Attacks via Progressive Answer Detoxification":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the vulnerability of Large Language Models (LLMs) to jailbreak attacks, where crafted prompts manipulate the model into generating harmful outputs.  It argues that traditional safety alignment methods are limited by their focus on initial generation steps and proposes a new framework called DEEPALIGN.  DEEPALIGN fine-tunes LLMs to progressively detoxify generated content by enhancing their inherent awareness of toxicity throughout the generation process.  This involves a hybrid loss function operating on hidden states and a method for generating semantically relevant, safe responses to harmful queries.  The paper claims state-of-the-art defense performance against various attack types while preserving utility.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The concept of progressively detoxifying the generated content rather than relying solely on initial token analysis is novel. The idea of using a hybrid loss function operating on hidden states to directly improve LLMs' awareness of toxicity is also a contribution.  However, contrastive learning techniques are not entirely new in the context of LLM alignment, so the *specific* formulation and its application to *progressive* detoxification constitute the incremental novelty.  The automatic data generation process for semantically relevant safe responses is also interesting, but builds upon existing data augmentation techniques.

*   **Significance:** If the claimed performance holds up, the work is significant. Jailbreak attacks are a major concern for LLM deployment.  A robust defense mechanism that doesn't significantly sacrifice utility is highly valuable.  The focus on dynamic, context-aware mitigation is a step forward from static approaches. The paper addresses a clear and pressing problem.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The progressive approach is intuitively appealing and aligns with the autoregressive nature of LLMs.
    *   The hybrid loss function is well-motivated and designed to address a specific weakness.
    *   The comprehensive evaluation across multiple LLMs and attack types adds credibility.
    *   The adaptive attack provides a strong evaluation by directly challenging the defense at its targeted layers.
    *   The authors clearly identify and address limitations of conventional safety alignment.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The details of the automatic data generation process (safe response creation) are slightly unclear. More details about the prompts and models used for generating the "safe" data would improve replicability.
    *   The evaluation, while thorough, could be more transparent about computational resources.
    *   The discussion of limitations is good, but the real-world applicability is unclear. What is the inference speed overhead introduced by the Detoxify Loss? This must be explored.
    *   While the method lowers ASR, does it inadvertently introduce new, unexpected behaviors or biases? A deeper analysis is required, beyond just refusal rates and utility.
    *   The description of hyperparameter tuning could be improved.

*   **Impact:** If the performance claims hold true and the approach can be scaled efficiently, this work could influence the design of future LLM safety mechanisms.  It offers a promising direction for more robust and context-aware defenses. The analysis of hidden states provides valuable insight into the vulnerabilities of LLMs and possible defense strategies.

*   **Rigorous Rationale:** While the approach is compelling and the results are promising, the paper isn't a paradigm shift. The underlying concepts (contrastive learning, attention, data augmentation) are well-established. The paper combines these elements in a novel way and demonstrates empirical improvements, but the novelty is incremental rather than revolutionary.  The long-term impact will depend on the generalizability and scalability of the method. However, the thorough empirical evaluation provides a sound base to build upon.

Score: 7

**Justification for the Score:**  A score of 7 reflects the paper's significant, but incremental, contribution to the field. It's well-motivated, has a strong empirical evaluation (with certain aspects potentially improved) and addresses a highly relevant problem. However, the core techniques are built upon existing methods, and more work is needed to assess its long-term impact and practical limitations.

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 7/10

### FastVID: Dynamic Density Pruning for Fast Video Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.11187v1)
- **Authors**: Leqi Shen, Guoqiang Gong, Tao He, Yifeng Zhang, Pengzhang Liu, Sicheng Zhao, Guiguang Ding
- **Abstract**: Video Large Language Models have shown impressive capabilities in video comprehension, yet their practical deployment is hindered by substantial inference costs caused by redundant video tokens. Existing pruning techniques fail to fully exploit the spatiotemporal redundancy inherent in video data. To bridge this gap, we perform a systematic analysis of video redundancy from two perspectives: temporal context and visual context. Leveraging this insight, we propose Dynamic Density Pruning for Fast Video LLMs termed FastVID. Specifically, FastVID dynamically partitions videos into temporally ordered segments to preserve temporal structure and applies a density-based token pruning strategy to maintain essential visual information. Our method significantly reduces computational overhead while maintaining temporal and visual integrity. Extensive evaluations show that FastVID achieves state-of-the-art performance across various short- and long-video benchmarks on leading Video LLMs, including LLaVA-OneVision and LLaVA-Video. Notably, FastVID effectively prunes 90% of video tokens while retaining 98.0% of LLaVA-OneVision's original performance. The code is available at https://github.com/LunarShen/FastVID.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "FastVID: Dynamic Density Pruning for Fast Video Large Language Models" addresses the computational cost of running Video Large Language Models (Video LLMs) by proposing a novel inference-time token pruning technique. The core idea is to exploit spatiotemporal redundancy in video data.  FastVID comprises two main components: Dynamic Temporal Segmentation (DySeg), which adaptively divides the video into temporally coherent segments, and Density Spatiotemporal Pruning (STPrune), which reduces redundant tokens within each segment using a density-based merging strategy and attention-based selection for salient details.  The authors demonstrate that FastVID achieves state-of-the-art performance on various video understanding benchmarks while significantly reducing the number of tokens processed by the LLM. The paper is accompanied by code.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**

    *   The idea of exploiting spatiotemporal redundancy in video data for LLM acceleration is not entirely new. Prior work has explored both spatial and temporal compression, but the novelty of this paper lies in the *combination* of the dynamic temporal segmentation *and* the density-based pruning within each segment. This combined approach, along with the careful consideration of positional information (especially relevant for LLaVA-Video), represents a novel contribution.
    *   The density-based token merging is inspired by existing density clustering techniques, but its adaptation and application to video token pruning, with the addition of attention-based refinement for salient details, adds incremental novelty. The focus on retaining positional information, especially in the context of LLaVA-Video's architecture, is a well-motivated design choice.
    *   The ablation studies systematically validate the contributions of individual components (DySeg, DTM, ATS) and parameter choices, strengthening the argument for the effectiveness of the proposed method.

*   **Significance:**

    *   The results presented demonstrate a substantial reduction in computational overhead (up to 90% token pruning) while maintaining high performance (approaching or exceeding vanilla performance). This is practically significant for deploying Video LLMs in resource-constrained environments.
    *   The comprehensive experimental evaluation across multiple benchmarks and Video LLM architectures (LLaVA-OneVision and LLaVA-Video) lends credibility to the generalizability of the approach.  The inclusion of long-video benchmarks is particularly relevant as many existing methods struggle with long sequences.
    *   The work addresses a key challenge in the field of Video LLMs: making them more efficient and practical. By focusing on inference-time acceleration, the method can be readily applied to existing models without requiring retraining. The plug-and-play nature of the design is a significant advantage.
    *   The clear writing and readily available code contribute to the reproducibility and potential impact of the work. Other researchers can easily build upon this approach.
    *   While the individual components (temporal segmentation and density-based merging) are not revolutionary on their own, their synergistic combination in the context of Video LLMs and the focus on maintaining visual and temporal integrity is significant.
    * The adaptation of the approach for LLaVA-Video which used newline tokens based on positional information is a key contribution which many other pruning methods do not explicitly consider.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   While the paper provides a good ablation study, a more detailed analysis of the *types* of videos or scenes where FastVID performs best or worst would be beneficial.  Understanding the limitations of the method is crucial for practical applications.
    *   The hyperparameter tuning seems relatively basic. Exploring more sophisticated hyperparameter optimization techniques might further improve performance.
    * The paper could benefit from a qualitative analysis of the tokens that are pruned and retained by FastVID. Visualizing the selected tokens could provide insights into the method's behavior and its ability to preserve key visual features.

**Score:** 8

**Justification:**

FastVID is a solid contribution to the field of Video LLMs.  It's not a groundbreaking theoretical advance, but it provides a practical and effective solution to a critical problem: the high computational cost of Video LLM inference. The novelty lies in the combination of dynamic temporal segmentation and density-based pruning, along with careful consideration of video-specific characteristics. The experimental results are compelling, demonstrating significant efficiency gains while maintaining strong performance. The readily available code enhances the potential impact of the work.  While the method isn't without limitations (e.g., need for more detailed analysis of failure cases), its strengths outweigh its weaknesses.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Cross-Modal Learning for Music-to-Music-Video Description Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.11190v1)
- **Authors**: Zhuoyuan Mao, Mengjie Zhao, Qiyu Wu, Zhi Zhong, Wei-Hsiang Liao, Hiromi Wakaki, Yuki Mitsufuji
- **Abstract**: Music-to-music-video generation is a challenging task due to the intrinsic differences between the music and video modalities. The advent of powerful text-to-video diffusion models has opened a promising pathway for music-video (MV) generation by first addressing the music-to-MV description task and subsequently leveraging these models for video generation. In this study, we focus on the MV description generation task and propose a comprehensive pipeline encompassing training data construction and multimodal model fine-tuning. We fine-tune existing pre-trained multimodal models on our newly constructed music-to-MV description dataset based on the Music4All dataset, which integrates both musical and visual information. Our experimental results demonstrate that music representations can be effectively mapped to textual domains, enabling the generation of meaningful MV description directly from music inputs. We also identify key components in the dataset construction pipeline that critically impact the quality of MV description and highlight specific musical attributes that warrant greater focus for improved MV description generation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the task of generating music video (MV) descriptions from music. It proposes a pipeline involving data construction and multimodal model fine-tuning. The authors construct a new music-to-MV description dataset based on the Music4All dataset, integrating music and visual information. They fine-tune pre-trained multimodal language models (LLMs) using this dataset and explore the impact of various data sources (music, genre tags, MV type tags, lyrics understanding text) on the quality of generated MV descriptions. Experiments demonstrate that music representations can be mapped to textual domains for meaningful MV description generation and identifies key components in the dataset construction pipeline impacting MV description quality.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper demonstrates some level of novelty by focusing on the music-to-MV *description* generation as an intermediate step for music-to-video generation.  While MV generation is not new, the approach of first generating descriptions and then using text-to-video models adds a modular approach to the problem. The creation of a specific dataset tailored for this task (using Music4All and enhancing it with OpenMU) and the systematic evaluation of different input modalities contributes to novelty. The exploration of incorporating music genre tags and MV type tags is another source of novelty beyond relying solely on lyrics.

*   **Significance:** The significance lies in enabling better control and interpretability in music-to-video generation. By generating a text-based description first, users or systems can potentially refine the video's content. The study provides insights into which musical attributes are most important for generating meaningful MV descriptions. The curated dataset serves as a valuable resource for future research. The modular nature facilitates use of pre-existing music understanding models and off-the-shelf text-to-video generators.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition and focus on a specific sub-task.
    *   Comprehensive pipeline encompassing data construction, model fine-tuning, and ablation studies.
    *   Systematic evaluation of different input modalities and their impact on MV description quality.
    *   Creation of a new music-to-MV description dataset.
    *  Modularity and applicability to different music understanding and video generation models.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The evaluation relies on automatic metrics (BLEU, ROUGE, BERTScore), which may not perfectly capture the semantic coherence and artistic quality of the generated descriptions. Subjective evaluations could provide a more complete picture.
    *   Limited exploration of temporal alignment between music and the generated MV descriptions.
    *   The reliance on LLMs for captioning within the data construction pipeline introduces a potential bias and dependency on the LLM's capabilities.
    *  The improvement over baseline NEXT-GPT, although significant, might be partially attributable to domain specialization via fine-tuning. The contribution of individual components of the pipeline isn't entirely disentangled beyond ablation.

*   **Potential Influence:**
    The work can influence future research by:
    *   Providing a benchmark dataset and evaluation protocol for music-to-MV description generation.
    *   Highlighting the importance of different musical attributes for MV description generation.
    *   Encouraging the development of more sophisticated multimodal models that can effectively capture the relationship between music and visual content.
    * Facilitating improved controllability of music video generation through descriptive interventions.

* **Areas for Improvement:**
    * Conduct human evaluation of the generated MV descriptions to gauge coherence and quality.
    * Evaluate the system across different music genres.
    * Extend the pipeline to address the temporal aspects of the music.

**Overall:**
The paper presents a well-executed study on a relevant problem. The approach has merit, the experiments are systematic, and the findings provide valuable insights. However, the limitations in evaluation and the incremental nature of the novelty compared to existing MV generation work keep it from being a truly groundbreaking contribution.

**Score: 6.5**

**Justification:** The paper is a solid and competent contribution to the field of music-to-video generation. It is novel in its approach focusing specifically on the intermediate step of generating MV descriptions. The creation and analysis of the dataset is valuable. However, the overall impact and novelty is limited, particularly as the improvements are incremental within the context of existing literature. The lack of human evaluation holds the work back from a higher score. The score reflects the technical merit, the reasonably well-defined research question, but also the areas needing further attention.
- **Classification**: cs.SD
- **Score**: 6/10

### Provenance Detection for AI-Generated Images: Combining Perceptual Hashing, Homomorphic Encryption, and AI Detection Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.11195v1)
- **Authors**: Shree Singhi, Aayan Yadav, Aayush Gupta, Shariar Ebrahimi, Parisa Hassanizadeh
- **Abstract**: As AI-generated sensitive images become more prevalent, identifying their source is crucial for distinguishing them from real images. Conventional image watermarking methods are vulnerable to common transformations like filters, lossy compression, and screenshots, often applied during social media sharing. Watermarks can also be faked or removed if models are open-sourced or leaked since images can be rewatermarked. We have developed a three-part framework for secure, transformation-resilient AI content provenance detection, to address these limitations. We develop an adversarially robust state-of-the-art perceptual hashing model, DinoHash, derived from DINOV2, which is robust to common transformations like filters, compression, and crops. Additionally, we integrate a Multi-Party Fully Homomorphic Encryption~(MP-FHE) scheme into our proposed framework to ensure the protection of both user queries and registry privacy. Furthermore, we improve previous work on AI-generated media detection. This approach is useful in cases where the content is absent from our registry. DinoHash significantly improves average bit accuracy by 12% over state-of-the-art watermarking and perceptual hashing methods while maintaining superior true positive rate (TPR) and false positive rate (FPR) tradeoffs across various transformations. Our AI-generated media detection results show a 25% improvement in classification accuracy on commonly used real-world AI image generators over existing algorithms. By combining perceptual hashing, MP-FHE, and an AI content detection model, our proposed framework provides better robustness and privacy compared to previous work.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of provenance detection for AI-generated images, aiming to distinguish them from real images and trace their origin. It proposes a three-part framework that combines: 1) a robust perceptual hashing model called DinoHash (derived from DINOv2) that is resilient to common image transformations, 2) Multi-Party Fully Homomorphic Encryption (MP-FHE) to ensure privacy during hash querying and storage, and 3) an AI-based content detection model as a failsafe mechanism when images are not found in the hash registry.  The paper emphasizes adversarial training for the perceptual hash to prevent hash collisions and aversions. Experimental results show improved bit accuracy compared to existing watermarking and perceptual hashing techniques, as well as better AI-generated media detection accuracy than current algorithms.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper demonstrates moderate novelty.  While perceptual hashing and AI-based forgery detection are not entirely new, the combination of DinoHash with MP-FHE and a failsafe detection model *is* a novel combination.  The adversarial training scheme for the hash model adds a layer of robustness.  The use of DinoV2 as a feature extractor may not be ground-breaking, but its specific adaptation and incorporation in this provenance system contributes to the novelty.

*   **Significance:** The significance lies in addressing a critical problem: securing the provenance of AI-generated content in a world where deepfakes and synthetic media are becoming increasingly prevalent.  The framework offers a promising solution that attempts to balance robustness, privacy, and accuracy. The experimental results, demonstrating improvements over existing methods, support the potential impact of this work.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Comprehensive approach: Tackles the problem from multiple angles (robust hashing, privacy, failsafe detection).
    *   Adversarial training: Addresses a key weakness in perceptual hashing by making the system more resilient to attacks.
    *   MP-FHE integration: Enhances the security and privacy of the system.
    *   Strong experimental results: Demonstrates improvements in key metrics compared to existing methods.
    * Addresses practical challenges: Acknowledges the limitations of watermarking and existing provenance detection methods.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Computational overhead of FHE: The paper acknowledges that the FHE component adds computational overhead, which could limit real-time applications.  The PoC implementation results show significant latency for even small databases. More discussion around optimizing FHE for scalability is needed.
    *   Dependency on content-producing organizations (CPOs): The framework relies on CPOs to generate and register hashes.  This raises questions about adoption, compliance, and coverage of all AI-generated content. What about content generated by individuals/smaller groups? A practical deployment plan is lacking.
    *   Evaluation Dataset: The focus appears to be the synthbuster data set. A wider set of datasets would improve generalizability claims.
    *   Scalability of the MP-FHE: While the paper mentions scaling using trustless distributed parties, the current reliance on complete re-encryption by each party is not clearly optimized and limits deployment.

*   **Impact:** If effectively scaled and widely adopted, the framework could significantly contribute to combating the spread of misinformation and protecting intellectual property in the age of AI-generated content. However, the dependency on CPOs and the computational cost of MP-FHE pose significant hurdles to widespread adoption.

*   **Justification of Score:**
Given the strengths and weaknesses, a score of **7** is justified. The paper addresses an important problem with a novel and well-engineered framework that demonstrates potential impact. The improvements in bit accuracy and AI detection rate are significant and provide a concrete foundation for future work. However, the practical limitations related to scalability and adoption prevent a higher score. Furthermore, certain claims around existing state-of-the-art algorithms are not thoroughly defended. Further research and development are needed to address these limitations and realize the full potential of the proposed approach.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Reinforcement Learning Outperforms Supervised Fine-Tuning: A Case Study on Audio Question Answering
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.11197v1)
- **Authors**: Gang Li, Jizhong Liu, Heinrich Dinkel, Yadong Niu, Junbo Zhang, Jian Luan
- **Abstract**: Recently, reinforcement learning (RL) has been shown to greatly enhance the reasoning capabilities of large language models (LLMs), and RL-based approaches have been progressively applied to visual multimodal tasks. However, the audio modality has largely been overlooked in these developments. Thus, we conduct a series of RL explorations in audio understanding and reasoning, specifically focusing on the audio question answering (AQA) task. We leverage the group relative policy optimization (GRPO) algorithm to Qwen2-Audio-7B-Instruct, and our experiments demonstrated state-of-the-art performance on the MMAU Test-mini benchmark, achieving an accuracy rate of 64.5%. The main findings in this technical report are as follows: 1) The GRPO algorithm can be effectively applied to large audio language models (LALMs), even when the model has only 8.2B parameters; 2) With only 38k post-training samples, RL significantly outperforms supervised fine-tuning (SFT), indicating that RL-based approaches can be effective without large datasets; 3) The explicit reasoning process has not shown significant benefits for AQA tasks, and how to efficiently utilize deep thinking remains an open question for further research; 4) LALMs still lag far behind humans auditory-language reasoning, suggesting that the RL-based approaches warrant further exploration. Our project is available at https://github.com/xiaomi/r1-aqa and https://huggingface.co/mispeech/r1-aqa.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores the application of reinforcement learning (RL) to the audio question answering (AQA) task, a multimodal problem requiring understanding and reasoning based on audio content.  The authors leverage the Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) algorithm with Qwen2-Audio-7B-Instruct, demonstrating state-of-the-art performance on the MMAU Test-mini benchmark.  Key findings include the effectiveness of GRPO for large audio language models (LALMs), the potential for RL to outperform supervised fine-tuning (SFT) with limited data, and that explicit reasoning processes (chain-of-thought) don't significantly benefit AQA in this context.  The paper concludes that LALMs still lag behind human auditory-language reasoning, motivating further exploration of RL-based approaches.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Novelty:** The paper contributes by specifically investigating RL in the audio modality, an area that has received less attention compared to visual multimodal tasks.  Applying GRPO to LALMs is a valuable exploration.
    *   **Empirical Results:** The empirical results on the MMAU Test-mini benchmark are strong, demonstrating state-of-the-art performance.  The comparison between RL and SFT, showing RL's effectiveness with limited data, is particularly significant.
    *   **Well-Defined Research Question:**  The paper addresses a clear research question: Can RL enhance reasoning in LALMs for the AQA task?
    *   **Reproducibility:** The authors make their project available on GitHub and Hugging Face, enhancing reproducibility.
    *   **Clear writing and organization**: The authors have made the paper easy to read and organized, following the usual format.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope of AQA Task:** Focus on a single-audio selection based AQA tasks limits the generalizability of the conclusion.

    *   **GRPO Algorithm Details:** The paper mentions GRPO but refers to other sources for details. While this is acceptable, a more self-contained description would improve readability and allow the reader to more easily assess its suitability to the audio AQA task.
    *   **Limited Ablation:** While the paper explores different prompts, it could benefit from more thorough ablation studies. For example, ablating different components of the reward function, or exploring alternative RL algorithms, or trying zero-shot CoT vs GRPO would give more insights.

    *   **Human comparison benchmark: **The comparison to human performance should have involved more people to get a statisticaly significant confidence interval.

    *   **Lack of insight into human AQA:** It would be useful to explain why the results between LLMs and humans for AQA performance are far off.

*   **Significance:**

    *   The paper demonstrates the potential for RL to improve reasoning in LALMs, particularly when data is limited. This is a significant finding for the audio processing community.

    *   The surprising result that explicit reasoning processes (CoT) didn't improve performance raises interesting questions about the nature of reasoning in LALMs and warrants further investigation.
    *   The work points towards future research directions, such as improving understanding and reasoning capabilities of LALMs, finding better chain-of-thought paradigm for audio modality.

**Overall Assessment:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field of audio processing and multimodal learning. While the scope is somewhat limited to a specific AQA task, the findings are significant and well-supported by empirical results. The use of RL, especially GRPO, demonstrates a promising direction for enhancing LALMs, and the counter-intuitive result about CoT encourages further research into reasoning strategies for audio. While more detailed ablation studies and discussion of the GRPO algorithm itself would strengthen the paper, the overall quality and significance are high.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:**

The score reflects the paper's valuable contribution and promising results, but also acknowledges the weaknesses related to the limited AQA tasks and scope of the exploration, a lack of ablation experiments, and the need for deeper insights into why human AQA results are far off from LLM's AQA results.

- **Classification**: cs.SD
- **Score**: 7/10

### LLaVA-MLB: Mitigating and Leveraging Attention Bias for Training-Free Video LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.11205v1)
- **Authors**: Leqi Shen, Tao He, Guoqiang Gong, Fan Yang, Yifeng Zhang, Pengzhang Liu, Sicheng Zhao, Guiguang Ding
- **Abstract**: Training-free video large language models (LLMs) leverage pretrained Image LLMs to process video content without the need for further training. A key challenge in such approaches is the difficulty of retaining essential visual and temporal information, constrained by the token limits in Image LLMs. To address this, we propose a two-stage method for selecting query-relevant tokens based on the LLM attention scores: compressing the video sequence and then expanding the sequence. However, during the compression stage, Image LLMs often exhibit a positional attention bias in video sequences, where attention is overly concentrated on later frames, causing early-frame information to be underutilized. To alleviate this attention bias during sequence compression, we propose Gridded Attention Pooling for preserving spatiotemporal structure. Additionally, we introduce Visual Summarization Tail to effectively utilize this bias, facilitating overall video understanding during sequence expansion. In this way, our method effectively Mitigates and Leverages attention Bias (LLaVA-MLB), enabling the frozen Image LLM for detailed video understanding. Experiments on several benchmarks demonstrate that our approach outperforms state-of-the-art methods, achieving superior performance in both efficiency and accuracy. Our code will be released.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LLaVA-MLB, a training-free approach for video understanding using large language models (LLMs). It tackles the challenge of limited token capacity in Image LLMs when processing videos by proposing a two-stage framework: sequence compression and expansion. The key innovation lies in mitigating and leveraging the positional attention bias inherent in Image LLMs, where attention is often overly concentrated on later frames. To mitigate this bias during compression, they propose Gridded Attention Pooling (GAPool). To leverage the bias during expansion, they introduce Visual Summarization Tail (VSTail). Experimental results demonstrate superior performance compared to existing methods in terms of efficiency and accuracy on several video understanding benchmarks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the targeted handling of positional attention bias in Image LLMs specifically for video understanding tasks. While attention mechanisms and token selection strategies are not new, the observation and specific mitigation/leveraging of this bias in a *training-free* video setting is a worthwhile contribution. GAPool appears to be a reasonable method to address the imbalance and VSTail a logical way to extract global summarization, given the observed bias.
*   **Significance:** The paper is significant for several reasons. First, it offers a practical solution to a key challenge in training-free video LLMs – the token limit and inherent attention biases. Second, it provides a method that balances spatiotemporal detail with computational efficiency. Third, the experimental results demonstrate clear improvements over state-of-the-art methods, suggesting the potential impact of the proposed approach. The focus on training-free methods is particularly appealing, as it reduces the need for large annotated video datasets and significant computational resources.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clearly identifies and articulates a problem (attention bias).
    *   Proposes sensible and well-motivated solutions (GAPool and VSTail).
    *   Provides strong empirical evidence to support claims.
    *   Focuses on a relevant and important area (training-free video LLMs).
    *   The efficiency analysis adds practical value to the contribution.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the attention bias phenomenon is clearly shown in the figures, the underlying *reason* for its existence in Image LLMs is not deeply explored. A better understanding could lead to more robust solutions.
    *   The ablation studies are useful, but some parameters could have been more systematically explored (e.g., different grid sizes for GAPool combined with varying segment lengths).
    *   The paper doesn't explore the *types* of videos or queries where the proposed method provides the most benefit, hindering a deeper understanding of the method's limitations.
    *   The discussion regarding limitations section is somewhat weak, particularly regarding limitations of query-relevant token selection; this point needs to be clarified.
*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in training-free video LLMs by highlighting the importance of addressing attention biases and by offering a practical and effective approach for mitigating and leveraging them. The findings could also be relevant to other applications where Image LLMs are used to process sequential data.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper makes a novel and significant contribution to the field of training-free video understanding by addressing the positional attention bias in image LLMs. The proposed GAPool and VSTail techniques are well-motivated and empirically validated. The paper is well-written, clearly explains the problem and solutions, and provides sufficient experimental evidence to support its claims. While some aspects of the experimental design and analysis could be improved, the overall quality of the work is high, and the paper has the potential to influence future research in this area.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Technologies on Effectiveness and Efficiency: A Survey of State Spaces Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.11224v1)
- **Authors**: Xingtai Lv, Youbang Sun, Kaiyan Zhang, Shang Qu, Xuekai Zhu, Yuchen Fan, Yi Wu, Ermo Hua, Xinwei Long, Ning Ding, Bowen Zhou
- **Abstract**: State Space Models (SSMs) have emerged as a promising alternative to the popular transformer-based models and have been increasingly gaining attention. Compared to transformers, SSMs excel at tasks with sequential data or longer contexts, demonstrating comparable performances with significant efficiency gains. In this survey, we provide a coherent and systematic overview for SSMs, including their theoretical motivations, mathematical formulations, comparison with existing model classes, and various applications. We divide the SSM series into three main sections, providing a detailed introduction to the original SSM, the structured SSM represented by S4, and the selective SSM typified by Mamba. We put an emphasis on technicality, and highlight the various key techniques introduced to address the effectiveness and efficiency of SSMs. We hope this manuscript serves as an introduction for researchers to explore the theoretical foundations of SSMs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

This paper provides a comprehensive survey of State Space Models (SSMs), focusing on their evolution, theoretical underpinnings, mathematical formulations, connections to other model classes (RNNs, CNNs, Transformers), and applications.  The survey structures the SSM landscape into three main stages: the original SSM, structured SSMs represented by S4, and selective SSMs exemplified by Mamba. It highlights key techniques that contribute to both the effectiveness and efficiency of SSMs, covering discretization methods, structural parameterizations, and hardware-aware optimizations. The survey also delves into the applications of SSMs across diverse domains like video processing, molecular modeling, speech/audio analysis, and more, providing an overview of numerous models and their specific uses.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The novelty of the paper resides primarily in its comprehensive and systematic organization of the vast SSM literature. While the individual models and techniques discussed are not new *per se*, their presentation within a unified framework, highlighting evolutionary relationships and technical connections, offers valuable insights. This is particularly useful given the rapid development and fragmentation of research in this area. The paper effectively synthesizes a diverse range of SSM variants and categorizes them based on key architectural innovations (structured vs. selective).

* **Significance:** The significance of this survey stems from the increasing importance of SSMs as a promising alternative to Transformers, especially for long-sequence modeling. By providing a coherent overview of the field, the paper lowers the barrier to entry for new researchers and practitioners, facilitates comparisons between different approaches, and helps identify potential avenues for future research. The detailed discussion of key techniques and the categorization of SSMs into different stages are helpful for understanding the development of the field.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Comprehensiveness:** The survey covers a wide range of SSM models and techniques.
    * **Systematic Organization:** The division into three main sections (original SSM, structured SSM, selective SSM) provides a clear and logical structure.
    * **Technical Detail:** The paper includes mathematical formulations and detailed descriptions of key techniques.
    * **Breadth of Applications:** The survey covers a diverse range of applications of SSMs.
    * **Timeliness:** The survey focuses on recent developments in the field, including Mamba and its variants.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited Critical Analysis:** While the survey provides an overview of different SSM models and techniques, it lacks in-depth critical analysis and comparison. For example, it would be helpful to have a more detailed discussion of the trade-offs between different design choices.
    * **Lack of Evaluation Metrics Comparison:**  The survey mentions that the performance of different models across tasks varies significantly; a section providing a table comparing various models on specific tasks along with performance metrics would make a substantial addition.
    * **Limited Discussion of Limitations:** While it acknowledges certain limitations, such as the struggle to fit complex data and some architecture's dependency on parallelisation, more depth in exploring the shortcomings of various SSMs, and how these can be circumvented, would improve this paper.
    * **Descriptive, not Prescriptive:** The paper is largely descriptive, summarizing existing work. It offers limited guidance on which SSM architectures or techniques are most suitable for specific problems or which research directions hold the most promise.

* **Potential Influence:** The survey has the potential to be a valuable resource for researchers and practitioners interested in SSMs. It could help to accelerate research in this area by providing a clear and organized overview of the field. The identification of key techniques and open challenges could also stimulate new research directions.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper is a well-organized and relatively comprehensive survey of a rapidly evolving field. However, its lack of in-depth critical analysis, limited discussion of limitations, and descriptive rather than prescriptive nature prevent it from reaching a higher score. While it provides a valuable overview, it doesn't offer substantial new insights or guide future research in a strongly directional way. Therefore, the score balances its strengths as a timely and comprehensive overview against its weaknesses as a more critical or forward-looking analysis.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### GKG-LLM: A Unified Framework for Generalized Knowledge Graph Construction
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.11227v1)
- **Authors**: Jian Zhang, Bifan Wei, Shihao Qi, haiping Zhu, Jun Liu, Qika Lin
- **Abstract**: The construction of Generalized Knowledge Graph (GKG), including knowledge graph, event knowledge graph and commonsense knowledge graph, is fundamental for various natural language processing tasks. Current studies typically construct these types of graph separately, overlooking holistic insights and potential unification that could be beneficial in computing resources and usage perspectives. However, a key challenge in developing a unified framework for GKG is obstacles arising from task-specific differences. In this study, we propose a unified framework for constructing generalized knowledge graphs to address this challenge. First, we collect data from 15 sub-tasks in 29 datasets across the three types of graphs, categorizing them into in-sample, counter-task, and out-of-distribution (OOD) data. Then, we propose a three-stage curriculum learning fine-tuning framework, by iteratively injecting knowledge from the three types of graphs into the Large Language Models. Extensive experiments show that our proposed model improves the construction of all three graph types across in-domain, OOD and counter-task data.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces GKG-LLM, a unified framework for constructing Generalized Knowledge Graphs (GKGs), encompassing Knowledge Graphs (KG), Event Knowledge Graphs (EKG), and Commonsense Knowledge Graphs (CKG). Recognizing that current approaches build these graph types separately, overlooking potential benefits from a unified approach, the authors tackle the challenge of task-specific differences. They create a comprehensive dataset by collecting data from 15 sub-tasks across the three graph types and categorizing them into in-sample, counter-task, and out-of-distribution (OOD) data.  The core of their approach is a three-stage curriculum learning fine-tuning framework, where a Large Language Model (LLM) iteratively learns from KG, EKG, and CKG data.  Extensive experiments demonstrate improved performance across all three graph types and various data settings (in-domain, OOD, counter-task).

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in several aspects: 1) proposing the novel task of building a unified framework for GKG construction, 2) creating and categorizing a comprehensive dataset spanning KG, EKG, and CKG tasks, and 3) developing a three-stage curriculum learning fine-tuning framework for GKG-LLM.  While existing works have explored KG, EKG, and CKG separately and some have explored using KGs to aid in EKG and CKG construction, the explicit goal of a unified architecture trained end-to-end is novel and provides a valuable contribution.
*   **Significance:** The significance stems from the potential for increased parameter efficiency and improved knowledge sharing among different graph types. The unified framework could lead to more efficient utilization of computing resources, better generalization capabilities, and deeper insights into the relationships between concepts, events, and commonsense knowledge.  The experimental results support these potential benefits, showcasing performance improvements across various datasets and settings.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Dataset:** The creation of a unified and categorized dataset is a significant contribution, providing a valuable resource for future research.
    *   **Well-Designed Framework:** The three-stage curriculum learning approach is logically sound and aligns with the progressive relationships between KG, EKG, and CKG.
    *   **Extensive Experiments:** The authors conduct thorough experiments, evaluating GKG-LLM on in-domain, OOD, and counter-task data, demonstrating its robustness and generalization abilities.
    *   **Clear Presentation:** The paper is generally well-written and provides sufficient details for replicating the experiments.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Incremental improvements:** While impressive, some of the performance gains, though consistent, might be considered incremental in certain specific tasks, even if they show considerable progress in others.
